```
import { DurableObject } from 'cloudflare:workers'
import { JobContainer, type ContainerLike, type ContainerStepResult } from './agent-container'
import type { StepRequest } from './job-step'

/**
 * [[REQ-646]] — the Durable Object one consultant job's container is reached
 * through, keyed by the job's id (`AGENT_CONTAINER` in `wrangler.toml`, with the
 * `[[containers]]` entry naming this class).
 *
 * A DOORWAY, LIKE EVERY OTHER CLASS IN A FILE THAT IMPORTS `cloudflare:workers`.
 * Everything it decides is {@link JobContainer}'s, which the node suites can load;
 * this only hands it the platform's container, this object's storage and the
 * `PLATFORM` binding the container's egress is sent to.
 *
 * IT HOLDS NO STATE BUT THE JOB'S TOKEN, and no compute: a Durable Object shares
 * its isolate's 128 MB with others ([[DOC-1]] item 7). The work is the container's.
 */
export class AgentContainer extends DurableObject<Record<string, unknown>> {
  private readonly job: JobContainer

  constructor(ctx: DurableObjectState, env: Record<string, unknown>) {
    super(ctx, env)
    if (!ctx.container) throw new Error('AgentContainer is bound without a [[containers]] entry')
    this.job = new JobContainer(ctx.container as unknown as ContainerLike, ctx.storage, env, env.PLATFORM)
  }

  /** Run one step of the job in its container. */
  runStep(request: StepRequest): Promise<ContainerStepResult> {
    return this.job.runStep(request)
  }

  /** Whether `token` is this job's token: asked by `PlatformApi` on every call. */
  verify(token: string): Promise<boolean> {
    return this.job.verify(token)
  }
}
```

```
import { boundNames, EGRESS_SECRETS, HELD_SECRET } from './platform-wire'
import type { StepAnswer, StepRequest } from './job-step'
import type { ExitReason, StepCompute } from './turn-log'

/**
 * [[REQ-646]] — ONE JOB'S CONTAINER: started on demand, asked to run each step,
 * destroyed when the job ends ([[DOC-86]] §3).
 *
 * The `AgentContainer` Durable Object (`agent-container-do.ts`, workerd only) is a
 * thin doorway onto {@link JobContainer}; everything it decides is here, where the
 * node suites can drive it against a stand-in for the platform's container.
 *
 * THE CONTAINER IS DISPOSABLE AND HOLDS NOTHING. It is started with the job's id,
 * a token minted for this job, and the env the host needs that is not a
 * credential ({@link containerEnv}); its HTTP to the platform's address goes to the
 * internal entrypoint, which is the only way it reaches D1, R2 and the junction.
 * A container that dies fails the step it was running and the Workflow retries
 * it; the retry starts a fresh one, which rebuilds what it needs from the
 * junction, D1 and R2.
 */

/** The port the container's server listens on. */
export const CONTAINER_PORT = 8080

/**
 * The address the container sends platform calls to. An IP literal, so the call
 * needs no name resolution inside the container. HTTP to it, and only to it, is
 * intercepted and handed to `PlatformApi`; every other request the container
 * makes (the model, a page it captures) goes out directly.
 */
export const PLATFORM_HOST = '10.0.0.1'

/** The intercepted address, as `interceptOutboundHttp` takes it. */
export const PLATFORM_ADDR = `${PLATFORM_HOST}:80`

/**
 * The model keys a container is given as env: the one kind of secret that
 * travels. Every other credential stays on the Worker ({@link EGRESS_SECRETS}).
 */
export const MODEL_KEYS = ['ANTHROPIC_API_KEY', 'OPENAI_API_KEY', 'FAL_KEY'] as const

/**
 * The plain values the host reads off `env`, passed as they are. An allowlist and
 * not "every string on env", so a secret added to the Worker tomorrow does not
 * reach a container by default.
 */
export const CONTAINER_VARS = [
  'TENANT_ID',
  'PLATFORM_ADMINS',
  'MAIL_FROM',
  'APEX_HOST',
  'TURN_DIAGNOSTICS',
  'SPEND_CAP_USD',
  'RECORD_BUILDER_CASES',
  'STRIPE_MODE',
  'STRIPE_CONNECT_CLIENT_ID_TEST',
  'STRIPE_CONNECT_CLIENT_ID_LIVE',
  'DEVELOPMENT_TICKETS_URL',
  // Where the model is reached: an address, not a credential (a gateway, or the
  // container suites' scripted model).
  'ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL',
] as const

/** What a container is started with. */
export function containerEnv(env: Record<string, unknown>, job: string, token: string): Record<string, string> {
  const out: Record<string, string> = {}
  for (const name of [...MODEL_KEYS, ...CONTAINER_VARS]) {
    const value = env[name]
    if (typeof value === 'string' && value !== '') out[name] = value
  }
  // Present, so the host composes the capability, and worthless: the platform
  // attaches the real credential to the request on its way out.
  for (const name of EGRESS_SECRETS) {
    const value = env[name]
    if (typeof value === 'string' && value.trim() !== '') out[name] = HELD_SECRET
  }
  out.JOB_ID = job
  out.JOB_TOKEN = token
  out.PLATFORM_URL = `http://${PLATFORM_HOST}`
  out.PLATFORM_BOUND = boundNames(env).join(',')
  out.PORT = String(CONTAINER_PORT)
  return out
}

/** A fresh job token: 32 random bytes, hex. */
export function mintToken(): string {
  const bytes = crypto.getRandomValues(new Uint8Array(32))
  return Array.from(bytes, (b) => b.toString(16).padStart(2, '0')).join('')
}

/** Constant-time string comparison, for the job token. */
export function sameToken(a: string, b: string): boolean {
  if (a.length !== b.length || a === '') return false
  let diff = 0
  for (let i = 0; i < a.length; i++) diff |= a.charCodeAt(i) ^ b.charCodeAt(i)
  return diff === 0
}

/** What is known about a container that stopped answering. */
export interface ContainerExit {
  /** Its exit code, where the platform said. */
  exitCode?: number | null
  /** The platform's own words on why it stopped, where it gave any. */
  message?: string | null
  /** Whether the platform said it was killed for memory. */
  oomKilled?: boolean
}

/**
 * [[REQ-646]] — how a container that stopped answering ended. A memory kill is
 * `oom` — said outright, an `exceededMemory`/OOM message, or the SIGKILL exit
 * (137) that a memory kill leaves; a SIGTERM exit (143) or a clean exit after one
 * is `sigterm`; anything else is `crash`.
 */
export function exitReason(exit: ContainerExit): ExitReason {
  if (exit.oomKilled) return 'oom'
  if (exit.message && /exceeded\s*memory|out of memory|oom/i.test(exit.message)) return 'oom'
  if (exit.exitCode === 137) return 'oom'
  if (exit.exitCode === 143) return 'sigterm'
  return 'crash'
}

/** What the container's server says about one step it ran. */
export interface ServerCompute {
  peakMemoryMb: number | null
  cpuS: number | null
}

/**
 * What a step's run reports to the Workflow: plain data, because a Durable
 * Object's RPC carries an error's message and not its fields.
 */
export type ContainerStepResult =
  | { ok: true; answer: StepAnswer; compute: StepCompute }
  | { ok: false; message: string; compute: StepCompute }

/** The slice of the platform's container a {@link JobContainer} drives. */
export interface ContainerLike {
  readonly running: boolean
  start(options: { enableInternet: boolean; env: Record<string, string> }): void
  monitor(): Promise<void>
  destroy(error?: unknown): Promise<void>
  getTcpPort(port: number): { fetch(input: string, init?: RequestInit): Promise<Response> }
  interceptOutboundHttp?(addr: string, binding: unknown): Promise<void>
}

/** The slice of Durable Object storage a {@link JobCo
```

<!-- xgd-tool name="Bash" id="toolu_01KzKwgexDt1LdG4xQwiT651" turn="cbe975aa50484cd6b17779ab094e8237" ts="2026-11-06T01:07:54.995891+00:00" -->

**Result**

```
/**
 * [[REQ-646]] — THE HOST IN NODE, AS ONE JOB'S CONTAINER RUNS IT ([[DOC-86]] §3).
 *
 * One process per job. The `AgentContainer` Durable Object starts it with the
 * job's id, a token minted for the job, the model keys and the plain values the
 * host reads (`containerEnv`), then asks it to run the job's steps one at a time
 * over HTTP. Each step runs through `executeStep` — the code the suites run and the
 * same host the `1c` CLI runs in-process — over an `env` whose bindings are
 * proxies to the platform's internal entrypoint (`platform-wire.ts`). The process
 * holds no state the job needs: it rebuilds everything from the junction, D1 and
 * R2, and a fresh process picks up where a dead one stopped.
 *
 *   GET  /health   200 once listening
 *   POST /step     run one `StepRequest`; answers `{ answer, compute }`
 *
 * SIGTERM (a deploy, or a host move): the step in flight is abandoned, its
 * checkpoint is written to the junction and flushed, the request is answered
 * `{ checkpoint: true }`, and the process exits. The Workflow retries the step,
 * which resumes from the junction and does not count the stop as a failure.
 */
import http from 'node:http'
import { consultantHost, clientPerson } from '../src/router'
import { executeStep, type StepRequest } from '../src/job-step'
import { platformFetch, platformTransport, remoteEnv } from '../src/platform-wire'
import { checkpointJobStep, type HostDeps } from '../../../tools/generate/src/cli/ai/host-core'
import { chromiumLauncher } from './chromium'

/** What one step's process used: its peak resident set and the CPU this step spent. */
function usage(cpuBefore: number): { peakMemoryMb: number; cpuS: number } {
  const now = process.resourceUsage()
  return {
    peakMemoryMb: Math.round((now.maxRSS / 1024) * 10) / 10,
    cpuS: Math.round(((now.userCPUTime + now.systemCPUTime - cpuBefore) / 1e6) * 1000) / 1000,
  }
}

function cpuNow(): number {
  const now = process.resourceUsage()
  return now.userCPUTime + now.systemCPUTime
}

function send(res: http.ServerResponse, status: number, body: unknown): Promise<void> {
  return new Promise((resolve) => {
    if (res.writableEnded) return resolve()
    res.writeHead(status, { 'content-type': 'application/json' })
    res.end(JSON.stringify(body), () => resolve())
  })
}

function readBody(req: http.IncomingMessage): Promise<string> {
  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    const chunks: Buffer[] = []
    req.on('data', (c: Buffer) => chunks.push(c))
    req.on('end', () => resolve(Buffer.concat(chunks).toString('utf8')))
    req.on('error', reject)
  })
}

/** Start the job's server. Exported for the suites, which run it in-process. */
export function startJobServer(opts: {
  vars: Record<string, string | undefined>
  /** Exit the process: `process.exit` in the container. */
  exit: (code: number) => void
  port?: number
}): Promise<{ server: http.Server; port: number; stop(signal: string): Promise<void> }> {
  const vars = Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(opts.vars).filter((e): e is [string, string] => typeof e[1] === 'string'))
  const job = vars.JOB_ID ?? ''
  const token = vars.JOB_TOKEN ?? ''
  const base = vars.PLATFORM_URL ?? ''
  if (job === '' || token === '' || base === '') throw new Error('the job container was started without JOB_ID, JOB_TOKEN and PLATFORM_URL')
  const direct = globalThis.fetch.bind(globalThis)
  const transport = platformTransport(base, job, token, direct)
  // THE PROCESS'S `fetch`: an account or provider API goes through the platform,
  // which attaches the credential this process is never given.
  globalThis.fetch = platformFetch(transport, vars, direct)
  const bound = (vars.PLATFORM_BOUND ?? '').split(',').filter((name) => name !== '')
  const env = remoteEnv(transport, bound, vars) as never
  const launch = chromiumLauncher(vars.CHROMIUM_PATH)
  let inflight: { request: StepRequest; deps: HostDeps | null; res: http.ServerResponse; cpu: number } | null = null
  let stopping = false

  const server = http.createServer((req, res) => {
    void (async () => {
      if (req.method === 'GET' && req.url === '/health') return send(res, stopping ? 503 : 200, { ok: !stopping })
      if (req.method !== 'POST' || req.url !== '/step') return send(res, 404, { error: 'no such route' })
      if (stopping) return send(res, 503, { error: 'stopping' })
      if (inflight) return send(res, 409, { error: 'a step is already running in this container' })
      const request = JSON.parse(await readBody(req)) as StepRequest
      if (request.job?.id !== job) return send(res, 403, { error: 'this container runs another job' })
      const cpu = cpuNow()
      const current = { request, deps: null as HostDeps | null, res, cpu }
      inflight = current
      try {
        const answer = await executeStep(request, {
          host: (scope, origin) => consultantHost(env, scope, origin, { launch }),
          person: (userId) => clientPerson(env, userId),
          running: (_request, deps) => {
            current.deps = deps
          },
        })
        if (!stopping) await send(res, 200, { answer, compute: usage(cpu) })
      } catch (err) {
        const message = err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)
        console.error(JSON.stringify({ event: 'job_step_failed', job, kind: request.kind, error: err instanceof Error ? err.stack ?? message : message }))
        if (!stopping) await send(res, 500, { error: message, compute: usage(cpu) })
      } finally {
        if (inflight === current) inflight = null
      }
    })().catch(() => send(res, 500, { error: 'the container could not read the request' }))
  })

  /** Stop: checkpoint the step in flight, answer it, and exit. */
```

**Result**

```
/**
 * [[REQ-306]] — the turn ledger: that a turn BEGAN, recorded where the turn
 * cannot reach it.
 *
 * THE FAILURE THIS MODULE EXISTS FOR. `streamTurn` hands back its `Response`
 * before `start()` runs, so the status line has gone out before any work begins.
 * An isolate killed mid-stream — `exceededMemory` is what happened, but a
 * CPU-time overrun, an eviction, or a limit nobody has met yet all produce the
 * identical shape — leaves the client a 200 with an empty body and no terminal
 * frame, and leaves the operator nothing whatsoever. The `catch` that renders a
 * readable error never runs. The `finally` that flushes the audit dies with it.
 * Every record this system keeps of a turn — the audit, the meter's row, the
 * pending record's close — is written by code that runs AFTER the model call, so
 * an uncatchable death is invisible by construction.
 *
 * SO THE ROW IS OPENED BEFORE THE STREAM AND CLOSED AFTER IT, and the whole
 * mechanism is that asymmetry. {@link openTurn} is awaited by the ROUTE, before
 * the `Response` exists; {@link closeTurn} runs in the same `finally` that
 * flushes the audit. A row that is still open is the record of a turn whose
 * isolate did not survive to write its own ending — which is the one fact
 * nothing inside that isolate could ever have recorded.
 *
 * IT MUST NEVER COST A TURN. Every function here swallows its own failure and
 * says so in its return value, on `previousTurn`'s reasoning in `host-core.ts`:
 * this is a safety net, and a net that fails the thing it was protecting has
 * made matters worse than having none. A ledger that cannot be written costs an
 * operator a record; refusing the turn would cost the customer their answer.
 */

import { newId } from '../../../tools/generate/src/store/ids'

/** What this module needs from the environment: a database, and nothing else. */
export interface TurnLogEnv {
  DB: D1Database
}

/**
 * The three words a closed turn can end with — `TurnOutcome` in `host-core.ts`.
 *
 * NOT A SECOND VOCABULARY. `turn_spend.outcome` and `pending_turn.status`
 * already carry exactly these, so the three records of one turn read side by
 * side with no translation. Restating the union rather than importing it keeps
 * this module free of the host's type graph; a UAT holds the two together.
 */
export type TurnLogOutcome = 'complete' | 'aborted' | 'error' | 'stalled' | 'lost' | 'restarted'

/**
 * [[BUG-249]] — the words only this ledger has.
 *
 * `stalled` is a turn the HOST ended because it stopped making progress and could
 * not be carried on; a stall the host recovered from closes `complete`, with the
 * stall in its detail. `lost` is what the watchdog ({@link sweepOpenTurns}) writes
 * over a row nobody closed — the derived judgement below, made durable, so no row
 * stays open forever. Neither reaches `turn_spend` or `pending_turn`, which keep the
 * host's three.
 *
 * [[BUG-288]] — `restarted` is a `lost` turn whose last sign of life falls at a
 * deploy: the Worker was replaced under it. Kept apart so the alarm's *something
 * is killing them* is said only of a death nothing explains.
 */
const OUTCOMES: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set(['complete', 'aborted', 'error', 'stalled', 'lost', 'restarted'])

/**
 * [[BUG-288]] — how long an unclosed turn may be SILENT before it is read as a death.
 *
 * SILENCE, NOT AGE. The ceiling this replaced measured how long a turn had been
 * open, on the premise that no turn ran past fifteen minutes; a consultant turn
 * that chains builder rounds runs for most of an hour while alive. Age says
 * nothing about whether a turn is running. Its last sign of life does: the
 * isolate running a turn stamps `alive_at` every {@link TURN_ALIVE_EVERY_MS}
 * while it is open, so a row that stops being stamped is an isolate that died.
 *
 * FIVE BEATS. Well above the cadence, so a slow write or a late timer is not a
 * death; well inside the ten minutes in which somebody is still wondering why
 * the builder stopped answering.
 *
 * ONE READER OWNS IT, WHICH IS WHY IT IS HERE AND NOT IN A PREDICATE. "Open" is
 * a fact about a row; "lost" is a judgement about an open row, and a judgement
 * spelled at each call site is a judgement that will differ between two of them.
 */
export const TURN_SILENT_AFTER_MS = 5 * 60 * 1000

/** [[BUG-288]] — how often a running turn stamps its row as alive. */
export const TURN_ALIVE_EVERY_MS = 60 * 1000

/**
 * [[BUG-288]] — how near a deploy a dead turn's last sign of life must fall to
 * read as ended by the restart. Either side: the version's timestamp is its
 * upload, the old isolate runs on for a grace period after it, and the turn's
 * last stamp can be up to a beat before it died.
 */
export const RESTART_WINDOW_MS = 2 * 60 * 1000

/**
 * A turn's entry in the ledger, or `null` where there is none to close.
 *
 * NULL IS AN ORDINARY STATE AND NOT A FAILURE. A deployment with no database,
 * and an open that could not be written, both produce it — and both must leave
 * the turn behaving exactly as it did before this module existed. The close is
 * then a no-op, which is what stops every caller having to ask whether it has
 * a ledger.
 */
export interface OpenTurn {
  readonly id: string
  /**
   * [[BUG-288]] — the turn is still running: stamp its row. The route calls it
   * every {@link TURN_ALIVE_EVERY_MS}. Never throws.
   */
  readonly alive: () => Promise<void>
  readonly close: (outcome: TurnLogOutcome, detail?: string | null) => Promise<void>
  /**
   * [[REQ-646]] — one job step's container, as it ended: folded into the row (the
   * peak kept, CPU and wall time summed, the latest exit reason, memory kills
   * counted). Never throws.
   */
  readonly compute: (step: StepCompute) => Promise<v
```

```
-- [[REQ-646]] — WHAT THE CONTAINER SPENT ON A TURN, AND HOW ITS STEPS ENDED.
--
-- A consultant job's rounds and builder passes run in a per-job container
-- ([[DOC-86]] §3). The question this platform kept being unable to answer was
-- *why* a turn stopped: memory, a deploy, or a crash. Each job step now records
-- what its container used, on the turn's own ledger row, so the answer is a
-- column read rather than a hunt through platform logs.
--
-- `peak_memory_mb` is the highest resident set any step of the turn reached,
-- `cpu_s` and `wall_s` are summed over the steps, `exit_reason` is how the
-- latest step's container ended (`ok`, `error`, `sigterm`, `oom`, `crash`), and
-- `memory_kills` counts steps whose container was killed for memory — an
```

```
import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process'
import { mkdtempSync, readdirSync, rmSync, statSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs'
import { DatabaseSync } from 'node:sqlite'
import os from 'node:os'
import path from 'node:path'
import { getPlatformProxy, unstable_dev, type Unstable_DevWorker } from 'wrangler'
import { sitePlan } from '../../apps/control-app/src/plan'
import { ticketStoreFor } from '../../apps/control-app/src/tickets'
import { d1r2SiteStore } from '../../tools/generate/src/store/d1r2-store'
import { editPageDecompose } from '../../tools/generate/src/cli/edit'
import { starterHomePage } from '../../tools/generate/src/cli/scaffold'
import { nextSlug, siteSeed } from './site-seed'

/**
 * [[REQ-646]] — THE CONTROL APP UNDER `wrangler dev`, WITH ITS JOB CONTAINERS.
 *
 * The real Worker (`worker.ts`, its own `wrangler.toml`), the real
 * `ConsultantTurn` Workflow, the real `AgentContainer` Durable Object, and the
 * job's real container — the image `container/Dockerfile` builds, run by Docker
 * the way `wrangler dev` runs it for local development ([[DOC-86]] §6). Nothing
 * inside the platform is replaced. The one double is the model, served over HTTP
 * (`model-server.ts`), because the container builds its own client.
 *
 * SEEDED THROUGH THE STORES, NOT THROUGH ROUTES. There is no route that creates a
 * site, so the tenant, its site, its Library and its products are written with
 * the store code itself, through `getPlatformProxy`, into the same local
 * persistence the dev server then serves.
 *
 * DOCKER IS REQUIRED, as it is for `bin/dev` and `bin/deploy`.
 */

type Untyped = any // eslint-disable-line @typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any

export const REPO = path.resolve(import.meta.dirname, '..', '..')
const APP = path.join(REPO, 'apps', 'control-app')
const WRANGLER = path.join(REPO, 'node_modules', '.bin', 'wrangler')

/** The image `wrangler dev` builds for the `AgentContainer` class. */
export const JOB_IMAGE = 'cloudflare-dev/agentcontainer'

export function docker(args: string[], opts: { allowFail?: boolean } = {}): string {
  try {
    return execFileSync('docker', args, { encoding: 'utf8', stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'] }).trim()
  } catch (err) {
    if (opts.allowFail) return ''
    throw err
  }
}

/** Whether a Docker engine answers. */
export function dockerRunning(): boolean {
  return docker(['info', '--format', '{{.ServerVersion}}'], { allowFail: true }) !== ''
}

/** The ids of the running job containers. */
export function jobContainers(): string[] {
  return docker(['ps', '--format', '{{.ID}} {{.Image}}'], { allowFail: true })
    .split('\n')
    .filter((line) => line.includes(JOB_IMAGE))
    .map((line) => line.split(' ')[0]!)
}

/** One frame of the prompt stream. */
export interface Frame {
  kind: string
  content?: string
  meta?: Record<string, unknown>
}

export interface JobRig {
  readonly origin: string
  readonly tenant: string
  post(route: string, body: unknown): Promise<Response>
  /** Read the database the dev server serves. */
  query<T>(sql: string, ...params: unknown[]): Promise<T[]>
  /**
   * The job records (`heartbeat` records carrying a `job_record` mark) on every
   * session junction written since `since`, read from the junction Durable
   * Objects' own storage, as the dev server persisted it.
   */
  jobRecords(since: number): Array<Record<string, unknown>>
  /** Seed more into the same stores, while the dev server runs. */
  seed<T>(work: (env: Untyped) => Promise<T>): Promise<T>
  stop(): Promise<void>
}

/** The stores' bindings, alone: what `getPlatformProxy` opens over the dev server's persistence. */
function seedConfig(dir: string): string {
  const file = path.join(dir, 'seed.wrangler.toml')
  writeFileSync(
    file,
    [
      'name = "job-rig-seed"',
      'compatibility_date = "2025-07-01"',

      'bucket_name = "1stcontact-sites"',
      '[[r2_buckets]]',
      'binding = "BLOBS"',
      'bucket_name = "1stcontact-material"',
      '',
    ].join('\n'),
  )
  return file
}

/** Parse a prompt response's server-sent frames. */
export async function frames(response: Response): Promise<Frame[]> {
  return (await response.text())
    .split('\n\n')
    .map((f) => f.trim())
    .filter((f) => f.startsWith('data:'))
    .map((f) => JSON.parse(f.slice(5).trim()) as Frame)
}
```

```
224:  it.runIf(DOCKER)('test_UAT_FC_REQ-646_container_reaches_stores_only_through_the_entrypoint', async () => {
270:  it.runIf(DOCKER)('test_UAT_FC_REQ-646_large_business_builder_pass_fits_the_container', async () => {
import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process'
import path from 'node:path'
import { afterAll, beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'
import { backendsDocument } from '../tools/generate/src/cli/ai/backends'
import { ticketStoreFor } from '../apps/control-app/src/tickets'
import { classify, labelOfMaterial } from '../apps/control-app/src/material'
import { createItem, readItemInput } from '../apps/control-app/src/shop'
import { BUILDER_ROLE } from '../tools/generate/src/cli/ai/roles'
import { calls, says, sentText, type ModelRequest, type ModelStep } from './support/scripted-model-client'
import { modelServer, type ModelServer } from './support/model-server'
import {
  REPO,
  docker,
  dockerRunning,
  frames,
  jobContainers,
  sectionText,
  sectionedSite,
  startJobRig,
  type Frame,
  type JobRig,
} from './support/job-container-rig'

/**
 * [[REQ-646]] — A CONSULTANT JOB'S WORK RUNS IN ITS OWN CONTAINER ([[DOC-86]] §3).
 *
 * Every case drives the control app as `wrangler dev` runs it for local
 * development: the real Worker, the real `ConsultantTurn` Workflow, the real
 * `AgentContainer` Durable Object and the job's real container, built from
 * `apps/control-app/container/Dockerfile` and run by Docker. The client posts a
 * message and reads the stream, exactly as the builder's chat does. The model is
 * the one double, served over HTTP to the container (`model-server.ts`).
 *
 * The container is killed with `docker kill` (a crash, or a memory kill) and
 * stopped with `docker stop` (SIGTERM, a deploy) — what the platform does to a
 * container, done to the real one.
 */


const WORKER_MODEL = backendsDocument.claude_builder.model
const DONE_LINE = 'The hero is rebuilt.'

const brief = (section: string) => ({
  note: `Rebuilding the ${section}.`,
  role: BUILDER_ROLE,
  goal: `Rewrite the ${section} section.`,
  accept: [],
  scope: [`home.json#${section}`],
})

const write = (section: string, text: string): ModelStep =>
  calls('set_l1', { page: 'home', section, path: '0', node: { kind: 'text', id: section, text, axes: { fontSizePx: 16 } } })

const report = (summary: string): ModelStep => calls('ReportResult', { summary, changed: [], decisions: [], passed: [], failed: [] })

const spoken = (seen: Frame[]): string => seen.filter((f) => f.kind === 'text').map((f) => f.content).join('')

/** Who the model is asked as, and the script each answers from. */
interface Script {
  consultant: ModelStep[]
  builder: (req: ModelRequest, n: number) => Promise<ModelStep | null> | ModelStep | null
}

let script: Script = { consultant: [says('Hello.')], builder: () => report('Nothing.') }
const asked = { consultant: [] as ModelRequest[], builder: [] as ModelRequest[] }

function play(next: Script): void {
  script = next
  asked.consultant.length = 0
  asked.builder.length = 0
}

const until = async (ok: () => Promise<boolean> | boolean, ms = 120_000): Promise<void> => {
  const end = Date.now() + ms
  while (!(await ok())) {
    if (Date.now() > end) throw new Error('timed out waiting')
    await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 250))
  }
}
```

```
/** Worker secrets that must never reach a container (the Worker has them for its own calls). */
const SECRETS = {
  CLOUDFLARE_DNS_TOKEN: 'dns-token-must-not-travel',
  RESEND_API_KEY: 'resend-key-must-not-travel',
  DEVELOPMENT_TICKETS_TOKEN: 'tickets-token-must-not-travel',
  STRIPE_SECRET_KEY_TEST: 'sk_test_must_not_travel',
}

let model: ModelServer
let rig: JobRig
const DOCKER = dockerRunning()

beforeAll(async () => {
  if (!DOCKER) return
  execFileSync('node', [path.join(REPO, 'apps', 'control-app', 'container', 'build.mjs')], { stdio: 'ignore' })
  model = await modelServer(async (req) => {
    if (sentText(req).includes('You are the talker.')) return says('Still building.')
    if (req.model === WORKER_MODEL) {
      const n = asked.builder.length
      asked.builder.push(req)
      return script.builder(req, n)
    }
    const n = asked.consultant.length
    asked.consultant.push(req)
    return script.consultant[Math.min(n, script.consultant.length - 1)]!
  })
  rig = await startJobRig({ modelUrl: model.url, vars: SECRETS })
}, 900_000)

afterAll(async () => {
  await rig?.stop()
  await model?.close()
})

async function ledger(sessionId: string): Promise<Array<Record<string, unknown>>> {

}

    expect(asked.builder.length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(2)
```

<!-- xgd-tool name="Bash" id="toolu_01FSXfWGUCEUgnRwhkCwwiG2" turn="cbe975aa50484cd6b17779ab094e8237" ts="2026-11-06T01:08:28.429497+00:00" -->

**Result**

```
     669 bin/deploy
assets
lib
migrate
secrets
step "platform fonts"
fonts_cmd=("$repo_root/bin/1c" fonts deploy --target "$target")
if [[ "$target" == "local" ]]; then
  fonts_cmd+=(--app "$(IFS=,; echo "${targets[*]}")")
fi
if [[ $no_fonts -eq 1 ]]; then
  fonts_cmd+=(--check)
  echo "  --no-fonts: checking only — nothing is delivered"
elif [[ $dry_run -eq 1 ]]; then
  fonts_cmd+=(--dry-run)
fi
DEPLOY_CAPABILITY_REPORT="$capability_report" "${fonts_cmd[@]}"

deployed=()
for app in "${targets[@]}"; do
  app_dir="$repo_root/apps/$app"
  name="$(worker_name "$app_dir" "$env_name")"
  [[ -n "$name" ]] || name="$app"

  step "$app → $name (--env $env_name, $target)"
  # BUILD FIRST ([[BUG-203]]): a deploy ships what it just built, never what an
  # earlier `bin/build` left lying in `dist-assets`, and a build that fails stops
  # the deploy before any migration is applied for code that will not ship.
  run_hooks assets "$app" "$app_dir" "$name"
  run_hooks migrate "$app" "$app_dir" "$name"
  run_hooks secrets "$app" "$app_dir" "$name"

  if [[ "$target" == "local" ]]; then
    ship_local "$app" "$app_dir" "$name"
  else
    # Composed once. --dry-run is appended to the SAME command line the real
    # deploy uses, so the two cannot drift apart.
    cmd=(npx wrangler deploy --env "$env_name")
    if [[ $dry_run -eq 1 ]]; then
      # [[REQ-646]] — a rehearsal builds no container image.
      cmd+=(--dry-run --containers-rollout=none)
    fi
    (cd "$app_dir" && "${cmd[@]}")
  fi
  deployed+=("$name")
done

# THE REPORT COMES BEFORE THE DEPLOYED LIST AND AFTER THE UPLOAD, deliberately:
# it describes what the thing just uploaded can and cannot do, which is only
# answerable once every hook has run. A deploy that stopped at a hook never
# reaches this — and does not need to, because the hook that stopped it printed
# the reason it stopped.
step "Capabilities"
DEPLOY_CAPABILITY_REPORT="$capability_report" \
  node "$repo_root/bin/deploy.d/lib/probe.mjs" report

step "Deployed"
for name in "${deployed[@]}"; do
  if [[ $dry_run -eq 1 ]]; then
    printf '  %-32s (rehearsed, not uploaded)\n' "$name"
  else
    printf '  %-32s %s\n' "$name" "$env_name"
  fi
done

if [[ $dry_run -eq 0 ]]; then
  if [[ "$target" == "local" ]]; then
    # [[BUG-307]], [[BUG-342]] — BRING EVERY DEV SERVICE UP ON THE NEW SNAPSHOT,
    # whatever state it was in. Wrangler's hot reload kept its startup asset
    # manifest, so a deploy alone left old code answering; and restarting only
    # what was running left a crashed worker down through a clean deploy.
    # `refresh` replaces what serves these apps (pidfile or not), starts whatever
    # is down, verifies each answers and the worker serves the snapshot's own
    # bytes, and prints `Dev is up: …` — or exits non-zero naming what is not,
    # which fails this deploy.
    # `DEPLOY_DEV_REFRESH_CMD` replaces the command (it is handed the app names),
    # for the same reason as `DEPLOY_BUILD_CMD`: a test that ships a snapshot must
    # not start or stop the operator's real dev services on the real ports.
    step "Bring the dev environment up on the new snapshot"
    refresh_cmd="${DEPLOY_DEV_REFRESH_CMD:-$repo_root/bin/1c dev refresh}"
    $refresh_cmd "${targets[@]}"
  else
    printf '\nNow prove it serves:  bin/smoke\n'
  fi
fi
```

## Bash sed -n 1255,1380p tools/generate/src/cli/dev.ts; grep -n "devRefresh\|devUp(" tools/generate/src/cli/*.ts | grep -v "^to…
<!-- xgd-tool name="Bash" id="toolu_01Mav6ohZehcPt989VeDmZs1" turn="cbe975aa50484cd6b17779ab094e8237" ts="2026-11-06T01:08:37.865123+00:00" -->

**Input**

```json
{"command":"sed -n 1255,1380p tools/generate/src/cli/dev.ts; grep -n \"devRefresh\\|devUp(\" tools/generate/src/cli/*.ts | grep -v \"^tools/generate/src/cli/dev.ts\" | head","description":"Read devRefresh implementation"}
```

**Result**

```
 * serving a deployed app must be a process this refresh started (so it read the
 * new snapshot at startup); and a service declaring a {@link DevService.probe}
 * must serve that asset with the snapshot's own bytes. A worker that kept its old
 * asset manifest fails that, which is how [[BUG-334]] went unnoticed.
 *
 * THE PROBE PASSES THE GATE LIKE THE OPERATOR ([[BUG-346]]). With Access on, an
 * unsigned GET can only ever be the gate's 401, which used to be reported as a
 * service not serving the snapshot. The probe now signs in through the local
 * simulator ({@link probeAccess}); when it cannot, or the gate still refuses the
 * signed-in request, the service is {@link DevRefreshOutcome.unverified} — named,
 * with the reason — and not a failure. Wrong bytes, and any other non-200, are
 * still failures. `devEnv` is the Worker's own env layering per app, injected
 * so a suite never reads the operator's secrets file.
 */
export async function devRefresh(
  ctx: DevContext & {
    apps: readonly string[]
    timeoutMs?: number
    fetchAsset?: FetchAsset
    devEnv?: (app: string) => Readonly<Record<string, string>>
  },
): Promise<DevRefreshOutcome> {
  const all = ctx.services ?? DEV_SERVICES
  const answers = ctx.answers ?? ((port: number) => portAnswers(port))
  const fetchAsset = ctx.fetchAsset ?? realFetchAsset
  const devEnv = ctx.devEnv ?? ((app: string) => readDevEnv({ appDir: path.join(ctx.repoRoot, 'apps', app) }))
  const scoped: DevContext = { ...ctx, services: all, only: undefined }
  const serving = all.filter((s) => s.snapshot !== undefined && ctx.apps.includes(s.snapshot))
  const restarted = serving.map((s) => s.name)
  const snapshots = ctx.apps
    .map((app) => readSnapshot({ repoRoot: ctx.repoRoot, app }))
    .filter((snap): snap is DevSnapshot => snap !== null)
  const base = { apps: ctx.apps, restarted, snapshots, services: all.map((s) => s.name) }

  const ports = new Set(all.map((s) => s.port))
  const occupied = devTable(scoped).listeners.filter((l) => ports.has(l.port) && l.origin !== 'this-checkout')
  if (occupied.length > 0) {
    return { ...base, occupied, down: null, up: null, failures: [], unverified: [], ok: false }
  }

  const failures: DevRefreshFailure[] = []
  const unverified: DevRefreshUnverified[] = []
  const down = await devDown({ ...scoped, only: restarted, adopt: true })
  if (!down.ok || down.occupied.length > 0) {
    for (const l of down.stillListening) {
      failures.push({
        name: l.service ?? '?',
        port: l.port,
        found: `pid ${l.pid} (${l.command}) was still answering on ${l.port} after SIGTERM, so nothing was started`,
        log: '',
        tail: [],
      })
    }
    return { ...base, occupied: down.occupied, down, up: null, failures, unverified: [], ok: false }
  }

  const up = await devUp({
    ...scoped,
    timeoutMs: ctx.timeoutMs,
    deploy: devDeploySkipped('this start follows the deploy that just ran'),
  })
  const failedNames = new Set(up.failed.map((f) => f.name))
  for (const f of up.failed) {
    failures.push({ name: f.name, port: f.port, found: f.detail, log: f.log, tail: logTail(f.log) })
  }

  const startedNames = new Set(up.started.map((s) => s.name))
  for (const s of all) {
    if (failedNames.has(s.name)) continue
    const log = path.join(devStateDir(ctx.repoRoot), `${s.name}.log`)
    const fail = (found: string): void => {
      failures.push({ name: s.name, port: s.port, found, log, tail: logTail(log) })
    }
    if (!(await answers(s.port))) {
      fail(`nothing is answering on ${s.port}`)
      continue
    }
    if (s.snapshot === undefined || !ctx.apps.includes(s.snapshot)) continue
    if (!startedNames.has(s.name)) {
      fail(`something was answering on ${s.port} after it had been stopped, so it was not replaced`)
      continue
    }
    const snapshot = snapshots.find((snap) => snap.app === s.snapshot)
    if (snapshot === undefined) {
      fail(`apps/${s.snapshot}/.dev-snapshot/ holds no snapshot to serve`)
      continue
    }
    if (s.probe === undefined || snapshot.assets === null) continue
    const want = path.join(snapshotDir(ctx.repoRoot, snapshot.app), snapshot.assets, s.probe)
    let expected: Buffer
    try {
      expected = fs.readFileSync(want)
    } catch {
      fail(`the snapshot has no ${s.probe} to check it against (${want})`)
      continue
    }
    const origin = `http://127.0.0.1:${s.port}`
    const url = `${origin}/${s.probe}`
    const access = await probeAccess(origin, devEnv(s.snapshot), fetchAsset)
    if (access.kind === 'unavailable') {
      unverified.push({ name: s.name, port: s.port, probe: s.probe, reason: access.reason })
      continue
    }
    try {
      const got = await fetchAsset(url, access.kind === 'session' ? { headers: { cookie: access.cookie } } : undefined)
      if (access.kind === 'session' && (got.status === 401 || got.status === 403))
        unverified.push({
          name: s.name,
          port: s.port,
          probe: s.probe,
          reason: `signed in as ${access.email}, the gate still answered ${got.status}${quoted(got.body)}`,
        })
      else if (got.status !== 200) fail(`${url} answered ${got.status}, not the snapshot's ${s.probe}`)
      else if (!got.body.equals(expected))
        fail(
          `${url} served ${got.body.length} bytes that are not the snapshot's ${s.probe} ` +
            `(${expected.length} bytes) — it is serving an older build`,
        )
    } catch (err) {
      fail(`${url} could not be fetched — ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)}`)
    }
  }

  return { ...base, occupied: [], down, up, failures, unverified, ok: failures.length === 0 }
}
```

```
 * DETACHED, WITH OUTPUT TO A FILE. `up` has to return to the operator's prompt,

export async function devUp(
  ctx: DevContext & {
    /** Per-service budget for the port to start answering. */
    timeoutMs?: number
    deploy?: DevDeployStep
  },
): Promise<DevUpOutcome> {
  const services = devSelection(ctx)
  const answers = ctx.answers ?? ((port: number) => portAnswers(port))
  const listeners = ctx.listenerPids ?? ((port: number) => listenerPidsOnPort(port))
  const sleep = ctx.sleep ?? realSleep
  const timeoutMs = ctx.timeoutMs ?? 60_000
  const deploy = ctx.deploy ?? devDeploy({ repoRoot: ctx.repoRoot })

  const started: DevStarted[] = []
  const alreadyUp: { name: string; port: number }[] = []
  const failed: DevUpFailure[] = []
  const served: DevServed[] = []

  const logDir = devStateDir(ctx.repoRoot)
  fs.mkdirSync(logDir, { recursive: true })

  for (const service of services) {
    if (await answers(service.port)) {
      alreadyUp.push({ name: service.name, port: service.port })
      recordServed(service, false)
      continue
    }
    const log = path.join(logDir, `${service.name}.log`)
    let pid: number | undefined
    // Set by the child's own `exit` event, which still arrives after `unref()`:
    // that drops the handle's claim on the event loop, not the handle. This loop
    // is awaiting sleeps, so the loop is alive to deliver it.
    let exit: { code: number | null; signal: NodeJS.Signals | null } | null = null
    try {
      const fd = fs.openSync(log, 'a')
      const child = spawn(service.argv[0], [...service.argv.slice(1)], {
        cwd: ctx.repoRoot,
        detached: true,
        stdio: ['ignore', fd, fd],
      })
      fs.closeSync(fd)
      child.on('exit', (code, signal) => {
        exit = { code, signal }
      })
      child.unref()
      pid = child.pid
    } catch (err) {
      failed.push({
        name: service.name,
        port: service.port,
        kind: 'spawn',
        detail: `could not spawn ${service.argv.join(' ')} — ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)}`,
        log: '',
      })
      continue
    }
    if (pid === undefined) {
      failed.push({
        name: service.name,
        port: service.port,
        kind: 'spawn',
        detail: 'spawned with no pid',
        log,
      })
      continue
    }
    const spawnedAt = Date.now()
    const startedAt = new Date(spawnedAt).toISOString()
    writeDevPidfile(ctx.repoRoot, { name: service.name, pid, port: service.port, startedAt, log })
    const outcome = await waitForPort(service.port, timeoutMs, answers, sleep, () => exit)
    if (outcome === 'up') {
      // THE PORT ANSWERING IS THE MOMENT THE LISTENER CAN BE ASKED FOR, and the
      // only moment: before it there is nothing holding the socket, and `up` has
      // just waited for exactly this. The pidfile is rewritten rather than written
      // once, so that a service that never answers still leaves the spawned pid
      // behind for `reap` to find ([[BUG-147]]).
      const listenerPid = chooseListenerPid(listeners(service.port), pid)
      writeDevPidfile(ctx.repoRoot, {
        name: service.name,
        pid,
        listenerPid,
        port: service.port,
        startedAt,
        log,
      })
      started.push({ name: service.name, pid, listenerPid, port: service.port, log })
      recordServed(service, true)
      continue
    }
    // THE LOG IS NAMED IN BOTH FAILURES, because a service that was spawned and
    // did not come up has already written the reason somewhere, and the
    // operator's next question is where. What differs is whether there is a
    // reason to look for at all.
    failed.push(
      outcome === 'exited'
        ? {
            name: service.name,
            port: service.port,
            kind: 'refused',
            detail:
              `${service.argv.join(' ')} exited ${exitDescription(exit)} within ` +
              `${Math.max(1, Math.ceil((Date.now() - spawnedAt) / 1000))} s without answering on ` +
              `${service.port} — it declined to start, and the reason it gave is in ${log}`,
            log,
          }
        : {
            name: service.name,
            port: service.port,
            kind: 'timeout',
            detail: `spawned as pid ${pid} but nothing answered on ${service.port} within ${Math.round(timeoutMs / 1000)}s — see ${log}`,
            log,
          },
    )
  }

  const stale = devStale(ctx)
  return { deploy, started, served, alreadyUp, failed, stale, ok: deploy.ok && failed.length === 0 }

  /**

      name: service.name,
      app: app.app,
      port: service.port,
      snapshot: readSnapshot({ repoRoot: ctx.repoRoot, app: app.app }),
      fresh,
    })
  }
}
```

```
  await fillOpenAPIConfiguration(config2, containersScope);
  const pathToDocker = getDockerPath();
  const boundDOs = new Set(
    config2.durable_objects.bindings.map((b9) => b9.class_name)
  );
  let imageRef;
  let maybeVersionInfo;

  for (const container of normalisedContainerConfig) {

        container,
        versionId,
        false,
        // dry runs will have already exited by this point
        pathToDocker,
        false
      );

      imageRef = { newTag: container.image_uri };

  addUserAgent(headers);
```

```
async function buildContainer(containerConfig, imageTag, dryRun, pathToDocker, verifyDockerIsRunning) {
  const imageFullName = containerConfig.name + ":" + imageTag.split("-")[0];
  logger2.log("Building image", imageFullName);
  return await buildAndMaybePush(
    {
      tag: imageFullName,
      pathToDockerfile: containerConfig.dockerfile,
      buildContext: containerConfig.image_build_context,
      args: containerConfig.image_vars
    },
    pathToDocker,
    !dryRun,
    containerConfig,
    verifyDockerIsRunning
  );
}
```

```
# it reaches D1, R2 and the junction, authenticated per job.
#
# REPEATED UNDER EVERY NAMED ENVIRONMENT, which inherits none of these: without
# them a deployed Worker has nowhere to run a job and refuses the message.
[[containers]]
class_name = "AgentContainer"
image = "./container/Dockerfile"
instance_type = "standard-1"
max_instances = 50

[[durable_objects.bindings]]
name = "AGENT_CONTAINER"
class_name = "AgentContainer"

[[migrations]]
tag = "v3"
new_sqlite_classes = ["AgentContainer"]

[[services]]
binding = "PLATFORM"
service = "1stcontact-control-app"
entrypoint = "PlatformApi"

# The consultant's work as a durable job ([[REQ-624]]).
#
# ONE INSTANCE PER CLIENT MESSAGE, ONE CHECKPOINTED STEP PER ROUND. A consultant
# turn — every builder pass included — used to run inside the browser's
# `POST /api/ai/prompt`, and an isolate killed mid-request killed the work with it.
# The request now starts an instance and follows its records on the junction; the
# platform retries a round whose step died, and the round resumes its open turn.
# The class is `ConsultantTurn` in `worker.ts`; the steps are the host's.
#
# [[REQ-646]] — EACH STEP'S WORK RUNS IN THE JOB'S CONTAINER (`AGENT_CONTAINER`
# above); the Workflow only schedules, retries and records. ABSENT, a deployed
# Worker refuses a message that would start a job: there is no in-isolate path.
# Only the `1c` CLI and the suites run steps in-process. REPEATED UNDER EVERY
# NAMED ENVIRONMENT, which inherits no bindings.
[[workflows]]
name = "1stcontact-consultant-turn"
binding = "CONSULTANT_TURN"
class_name = "ConsultantTurn"
name = "SITE_COORDINATOR"
class_name = "SiteCoordinator"

[[env.production.migrations]]
tag = "v2"
new_sqlite_classes = ["SiteCoordinator"]

# See the top-level block: restated rather than inherited, on purpose ([[REQ-646]]).
[[env.production.containers]]
class_name = "AgentContainer"
image = "./container/Dockerfile"
instance_type = "standard-1"
max_instances = 50

[[env.production.durable_objects.bindings]]
name = "AGENT_CONTAINER"
class_name = "AgentContainer"

[[env.production.migrations]]
tag = "v3"
new_sqlite_classes = ["AgentContainer"]

[[env.production.services]]
binding = "PLATFORM"
service = "1stcontact-control-app"
entrypoint = "PlatformApi"

# See the top-level block: restated rather than inherited, on purpose ([[REQ-624]]).
[[env.production.workflows]]
name = "1stcontact-consultant-turn-production"
binding = "CONSULTANT_TURN"
# production Worker, and `bin/deploy`'s own report names what it shipped — a name
# identical to production's would make a local deploy unreadable in a log. The
# cost is that miniflare keys Durable Object storage by script name, so
# SESSION_JUNCTION gets its own directory under `.wrangler/state`. That is
# harmless: the junction holds only the turn that is open right now, which a
# restart is already allowed to cost ([[REQ-307]]).
[env.dev]
name = "1stcontact-control-app-dev"
# Never uploaded, so this changes nothing today — and is stated anyway for the
# reason the top-level block states it (REQ-147): a security control that depends
# on remembering which keys inherit is one refactor from silently flipping back
# on. `workers_dev` IS inherited, so this is belt and braces.
workers_dev = false

# See the top-level block: restated rather than inherited, on purpose (BUG-37).
# LAST in this environment's bare keys, because a TOML table header ends the
# table above it.
[env.dev.observability]
enabled = true
head_sampling_rate = 1

# See the top-level block ([[BUG-168]]).
[env.dev.limits]
cpu_ms = 300000

# THE SAME VARS THE TOP-LEVEL BLOCK DECLARES, because that is what the old
# `wrangler dev` path reads and this environment replaces it rather than
# diverging from it. `SESSION_COOKIE_DOMAIN` is empty for the reason it is empty
# at the top level: this serves on `127.0.0.1`, which is nobody's cookie domain.
[env.dev.vars]
TENANT_ID = "biz_51a6746495c8057e886ff98d4208e6b9"
PLATFORM_ADMINS = ""
SERVICE_TOKEN_IDENTITIES = ""
ACCESS_DEV_OPEN = "1"
ACCESS_TEAM_DOMAIN = "lagrangefoundry.cloudflareaccess.com"
ACCESS_AUD = "c802fa9c4efc483e13f1c27d2d9a9ca3ff9336d55a014205bd1a63258260c74e"
tag = "v2"
new_sqlite_classes = ["SiteCoordinator"]

# See the top-level block: restated rather than inherited, on purpose ([[REQ-646]]).
[[env.dev.containers]]
class_name = "AgentContainer"
image = "./container/Dockerfile"
instance_type = "standard-1"
max_instances = 50

[[env.dev.durable_objects.bindings]]
name = "AGENT_CONTAINER"
class_name = "AgentContainer"

[[env.dev.migrations]]
tag = "v3"
new_sqlite_classes = ["AgentContainer"]

[[env.dev.services]]
binding = "PLATFORM"
service = "1stcontact-control-app-dev"
```

```
]

/**
 * was re-runnable or merely wasteful to re-run; that one is `ALTER TABLE ADD
 * COLUMN`, which is an ERROR the second time. So this check is now what stands
 * between a suite calling `applySchema` twice and a hard failure in setup.
 */
async function atHead(): Promise<boolean> {
  const { DB } = storeEnv()
  // `0049`'s last statement DROPS `inventory_items.collection_id` ([[REQ-651]]),
  // after creating `inventory_item_collections`; `0048` before it ADDS
  // `turn_log.memory_kills` ([[REQ-646]]), and `0041` DROPS `shop_settings`
  // ([[REQ-634]]). Head is the new table present, the dropped column gone, the
  // added one present, and that table gone. Columns are read from the tables'
  // shape, so a database with no `inventory_items` at all is not at head.
  const row = await DB.prepare(
    "SELECT SUM(type = 'table' AND name = 'inventory_item_collections') AS created, SUM(type = 'table' AND name = 'shop_settings') AS dropped, " +
      "SUM(type = 'table' AND name = 'inventory_items') AS items, " +
      "(SELECT COUNT(*) FROM pragma_table_info('turn_log') WHERE name = 'memory_kills') AS added, " +
      "(SELECT COUNT(*) FROM pragma_table_info('inventory_items') WHERE name = 'collection_id') AS column FROM sqlite_master",
  ).first<{ created: number | null; dropped: number | null; items: number | null; added: number | null; column: number }>()
  return row !== null && row.created === 1 && row.items === 1 && !row.dropped && row.added === 1 && row.column === 0
}

/** The tenant every fixture belongs to unless a test names another. */
export const DEFAULT_TENANT = 'tenant-default'

/** Register a tenant, so a handle can be taken for it. */
export async function ensureTenant(id = DEFAULT_TENANT, status = 'active'): Promise<void> {
  await d1r2SiteStore(storeEnv()).createTenant({ id, name: id, status })
}

/** A tenant-scoped handle, with the tenant registered first. */
export async function tenantStore(id = DEFAULT_TENANT): Promise<TenantSiteStore> {
  await ensureTenant(id)
  return d1r2SiteStore(storeEnv()).forTenant(id)
#!/usr/bin/env node
/**
 * `bin/migration-manifest` — the record of what an applied migration's bytes
 * were, and the check that they have not changed since ([[REQ-291]]).
 *
 * WHY THIS EXISTS. `d1_migrations` records a migration's NAME and the moment it
 * ran, and nothing else. A file that has been applied is therefore done forever
 * as far as wrangler is concerned, whatever is subsequently done to its
 * contents — which is how `0001_baseline.sql` came to be applied to production
 * on 2026-09-06 and then edited twelve times, leaving the applied schema and the
 * file on disk as two different databases wearing one name ([[EPIC-16]] §H).
 * `db/migrations/` already carried the rule — never edit an applied migration —
 * and the file that broke it is the file that documents it. What was missing was
 * not a firmer rule but a mechanism that notices a checked premise stop being
 * true.
 *
 * THE MANIFEST IS DELIBERATELY NOT DERIVED AT CHECK TIME. `db/migrations/manifest.json`
 * maps each migration's filename to the SHA-256 of its bytes, and is rewritten
 * only when somebody types the command below. That lag IS the mechanism: a hash
 * regenerated as a side effect of a build would always agree with the file it was
 * just read from and could never disagree with anything. Because it is committed,
 * `git diff` renders an edit to an existing migration as a CHANGED value — a line
 * somebody has to justify in review — and a new migration as an ADDED key, which
 * is ordinary and reads as ordinary.
 *
 * IT REPORTS, IT DOES NOT REPAIR. The remedy for a drifted migration is a
 * decision about data: rebaseline a database with nothing in it, or write a
 * corrective migration for one with customers. A script cannot make that call and
 * must not appear to, so `verify` prints both hashes and exits non-zero.
 *
```

```json
{"content":"/**\n * [[BUG-359]] — WHAT THE WHOLE JOB CONTAINER USES, AND WHAT IT IS ALLOWED.\n *\n * The step's peak memory used to be the Node server's own `maxRSS`, which leaves\n * out Chromium: Chromium runs as child processes and counts against the instance's\n * limit all the same (the live container used 1.7 GB while its row said 854 MB).\n * So the peak is now the container's:\n *\n *  - `cgroup` — the container's own memory cgroup, where there is one: its usage\n *    less the inactive file cache the kernel reclaims before it kills anything\n *    (what `docker stats` reports). A root cgroup has no `memory.current`, so a\n *    cgroup that would cover the whole machine (a VM's) is never read as this.\n *  - `process_tree` — otherwise, the summed resident set of the server's whole\n *    process tree, from `/proc` (or `ps` where there is no `/proc`).\n *\n * NEVER THE NODE PROCESS'S OWN `maxRSS`, which is the bug. The figure says which\n * of the two produced it.\n *\n * Sampled about once a second while a step runs, because neither source keeps a\n * per-step peak: `memory.peak` is the cgroup's lifetime high-water mark, and a\n * process tree has none.\n */\nimport { execFile } from 'node:child_process'\nimport { existsSync } from 'node:fs'\nimport { readFile, readdir } from 'node:fs/promises'\nimport path from 'node:path'\n\nexport type PeakMemorySource = 'cgroup' | 'process_tree'\n\n/** One way of reading the container's memory now, MB. */\nexport interface MemoryProbe {\n  readonly source: PeakMemorySource\n  /** The memory in use now, MB; `null` when it could not be read this time. */\n  read(): Promise<number | null>\n}\n\nconst MB = 1024 * 1024\n\n/** The usage, and the stat whose `inactive_file` is reclaimable, of a cgroup v2 or v1 hierarchy. */\nfunction cgroupFiles(root: string): { usage: string; stat: string; inactive: string } | null {\n  const v2 = path.join(root, 'memory.current')\n  if (existsSync(v2)) return { usage: v2, stat: path.join(root, 'memory.stat'), inactive: 'inactive_file' }\n  const v1 = path.join(root, 'memory', 'memory.usage_in_bytes')\n  if (existsSync(v1)) return { usage: v1, stat: path.join(root, 'memory', 'memory.stat'), inactive: 'total_inactive_file' }\n  return null\n}\n\n/** The container's cgroup, where one covers just the container; else `null`. */\nexport function cgroupProbe(root = '/sys/fs/cgroup'): MemoryProbe | null {\n  const files = cgroupFiles(root)\n  if (!files) return null\n  return {\n    source: 'cgroup',\n    read: async () => {\n      try {\n        const usage = Number((await readFile(files.usage, 'utf8')).trim())\n        if (!Number.isFinite(usage)) return null\n        const stat = await readFile(files.stat, 'utf8').catch(() => '')\n        const line = stat.split('\\n').find((l) => l.startsWith(`${files.inactive} `))\n        const inactive = line ? Number(line.split(' ')[1]) : 0\n        return Math.max(0, usage - (Number.isFinite(inactive) ? inactive : 0)) / MB\n      } catch {\n        return null\n      }\n    },\n  }\n}\n\n/** pid → [parent pid, resident KiB], for every process this one can see. */\ntype ProcessTable = Map<number, [number, number]>\n\nasync function procTable(): Promise<ProcessTable> {\n  const table: ProcessTable = new Map()\n  for (const entry of await readdir('/proc')) {\n    if (!/^\\d+$/.test(entry)) continue\n    try {\n      const status = await readFile(`/proc/${entry}/status`, 'utf8')\n      const ppid = /^PPid:\\s+(\\d+)/m.exec(status)\n      const rss = /^VmRSS:\\s+(\\d+)\\s*kB/m.exec(status)\n      table.set(Number(entry), [ppid ? Number(ppid[1]) : 0, rss ? Number(rss[1]) : 0])\n    } catch {\n      // A process that ended between the listing and the read.\n    }\n  }\n  return table\n}\n\nfunction psTable(): Promise<ProcessTable> {\n  return new Promise((resolve) => {\n    execFile('ps', ['-A', '-o', 'pid=,ppid=,rss='], { maxBuffer: 16 * MB }, (err, stdout) => {\n      const table: ProcessTable = new Map()\n      if (!err) {\n        for (const line of stdout.split('\\n')) {\n          const [pid, ppid, rss] = line.trim().split(/\\s+/).map(Number)\n          if (Number.isFinite(pid) && Number.isFinite(ppid) && Number.isFinite(rss)) table.set(pid!, [ppid!, rss!])\n        }\n      }\n      resolve(table)\n    })\n  })\n}\n\n/** The summed resident set of `root` and every process descended from it, MB. */\nexport function treeRssMb(table: ProcessTable, root: number): number | null {\n  if (!table.has(root)) return null\n  const children = new Map<number, number[]>()\n  for (const [pid, [ppid]] of table) {\n    if (pid === root) continue\n    const list = children.get(ppid) ?? []\n    list.push(pid)\n    children.set(ppid, list)\n  }\n  let kib = 0\n  const queue = [root]\n  const seen = new Set<number>()\n  while (queue.length > 0) {\n    const pid = queue.pop()!\n    if (seen.has(pid)) continue\n    seen.add(pid)\n    kib += table.get(pid)?.[1] ?? 0\n    queue.push(...(children.get(pid) ?? []))\n  }\n  return kib / 1024\n}\n\n/** The summed resident set of the process tree rooted at `root` (this process by default). */\nexport function processTreeProbe(root = process.pid): MemoryProbe {\n  const proc = existsSync('/proc/self/status')\n  return {\n    source: 'process_tree',\n    read: async () => treeRssMb(await (proc ? procTable() : psTable()), root),\n  }\n}\n\n/** The container's cgroup where one covers just it, else its process tree. */\nexport function containerMemoryProbe(): MemoryProbe {\n  return cgroupProbe() ?? processTreeProbe()\n}\n\n/** One step's peak, and how it was measured. */\nexport interface StepMemory {\n  peakMb: number | null\n  source: PeakMemorySource\n}\n\n/**\n * Sample `probe` from now until `stop()`, about every `everyMs`, keeping the peak.\n * `over` is
```

```
# The decision is printed and appears in the capability report.
#
# This replaces [[REQ-315]]'s "naming what you want moved": that was about not
# re-hashing 1.35GB on every code deploy, and change is now detected from the
# manifest instead of the bytes — so nobody has to remember the fonts after a
# font fix (EPIC-19, 2026-10-19: a fixed encoder sat undeployed behind a code
# deploy that never touched the dev stores). Staging the mirror (`1c fonts
# mirror`) is still the operator's step; deploy ships only what is staged.
#
#   --no-fonts                 skip the delivery for an emergency code-only
#                              deploy. The check still runs, and warns when the
#                              fonts are in fact stale.
#   --fonts --env production   the fonts and NOTHING ELSE: `1c fonts publish` —
#                              the cloud bucket, over the R2 REST API, with the
#                              CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN the rest of the release
#                              toolset already uses.
#   --fonts --env dev          `1c fonts seed` — the miniflare store `wrangler
#                              dev` reads, on this machine. The same prefix and
#                              the same key function, so `r2PlatformFonts(
#                              env.SITES)` serves local dev unchanged.
#
# Usage:
#   bin/deploy                       # every app, --env production
#   bin/deploy public-site           # one app
#   bin/deploy --dry-run             # rehearse everything
#   bin/deploy --env staging control-app
#   bin/deploy --env dev             # the LOCAL dev environment — a snapshot, not an upload
#   bin/deploy --no-fonts            # an emergency code-only deploy (warns if fonts are stale)
#   bin/deploy --fonts               # the platform font mirror only, to the cloud
#   bin/deploy --fonts --env dev     # …and to local dev, where the work is done
#   bin/deploy --secrets-file ~/Documents/secrets/1c.dev.env
```

```
---
name: cloudflare-account-id-var-breaks-kb
description: "The embedder's CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID + CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN pair is both-or-neither — complete it in the dev secrets file, never name either in wrangler.toml."
metadata: 
  node_type: memory
  type: project
  originSessionId: 201515cb-fe00-4b9f-b7e4-0bd324dcdb9d
  modified: 2026-09-16T23:01:56.243Z
---

`embedder.ts`'s `transportFor` treats `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` + `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`
as both-or-neither: half the pair throws `PartialAiCredentialError` **instead of**
falling back to the `AI` binding. The two contexts want opposite things, and
conflating them is the trap.
```

## Bash sed -n 1,100p tools/generate/src/cli/cloudflare-account.ts
<!-- xgd-tool name="Bash" id="toolu_014JFkMLzBqhvxaeJbfX6KJH" turn="cbe975aa50484cd6b17779ab094e8237" ts="2026-11-06T01:14:10.057117+00:00" -->

**Input**

```json
{"command":"sed -n 1,100p tools/generate/src/cli/cloudflare-account.ts","description":"Read account discovery helper"}
```

**Result**

```
/**
 * The Cloudflare account the release-time toolset works in.
 *
 * ONE CREDENTIAL FOR THE TOOLSET, NOT ONE PER COMMAND. `1c kb build` needs
 * Workers AI and `1c fonts publish` ([[REQ-312]]) needs R2, and both are the same
 * question of the same account — so the resolution lives here rather than being
 * answered twice. It was `kb.ts`'s private function until the font mirror needed
 * the same answer; extracting it is the alternative to a second copy that could
 * come to disagree about what an operator must supply.
 */

/** Cloudflare's REST root — the same API `bin/access-token` provisions through. */
export const CLOUDFLARE_API = 'https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4'

/** One account as `GET /accounts` reports it. */
interface CloudflareAccount {
  id: string
  name?: string
}

/**
 * The Cloudflare account to work in: named by the operator, or asked of the token.
 *
 * THE TOKEN ALREADY KNOWS. `GET /accounts` returns exactly the accounts a token
 * can see, so requiring the operator to look the id up and paste it alongside the
 * credential asks for a value the credential can answer for itself. This is the
 * rule `bin/access-token` has always followed (`resolve_account()` there).
 *
 * AN EXPLICIT ID SHORT-CIRCUITS THE CALL. It is the override, and a build that
 * has been given the answer has no business going to ask for it — which also
 * keeps a token too narrowly scoped to list accounts perfectly usable.
 *
 * SEVERAL ACCOUNTS GET NO GUESS. Taking the first would bind the work to
 * whichever account Cloudflare happened to list first, and the failure would not
 * be an error: it would be a successful run against the wrong account, discovered
 * much later. So they are named back to the operator instead.
 */
export async function resolveAccountId(apiToken: string, env = process.env): Promise<string> {
  const named = (env.CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID ?? '').trim()
  if (named) return named

  let accounts: CloudflareAccount[]
  try {
    // Cloudflare answers a refused operation with `success: false` under a 200 as
    // readily as it answers 4xx, so BOTH are checked. Reading only the HTTP status
    // would turn "you may not do that" into an empty account list, and an empty
    // list is reported below as a scope problem the operator does not have.
    const response = await fetch(`${CLOUDFLARE_API}/accounts`, {
      headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${apiToken}` },
    })
    const payload = (await response.json().catch(() => null)) as {
      success?: boolean
      result?: CloudflareAccount[]
      errors?: { message?: string }[]
    } | null
    if (!response.ok || !payload?.success) {
      const errors = (payload?.errors ?? []).map((e) => e.message ?? '?').join('; ')
      throw new Error(errors || `HTTP ${response.status}`)
    }
    accounts = payload.result ?? []
  } catch (err) {
    // The one failure that would otherwise read as broken work when the credential
    // is fine. Listing accounts is a permission of its own, so a token scoped
    // narrowly can do its job and still be refused here — and the way past it is
    // the override, not a new token.
    throw new Error(
      'Could not ask Cloudflare which account this API token belongs to ' +
        `(GET /accounts: ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)}). ` +
        'Listing accounts is a separate permission, so a narrowly scoped token can ' +
        'do its job and still be refused here — the credential is not necessarily ' +
        'wrong. Set CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID to name the account outright.',
    )
  }

  if (accounts.length === 1) return accounts[0].id
  if (accounts.length === 0) {
    throw new Error(
      'This CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN can see no Cloudflare accounts, so there is no ' +
        "account to work in — check the token's scope, or set CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID " +
        'to name the account explicitly.',
    )
  }
  const seen = accounts.map((a) => `${a.name ?? '?'} (${a.id})`).join(', ')
  throw new Error(
    `This CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN sees ${accounts.length} accounts; set ` +
      'CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID to say which one this work belongs in. ' +
      `Saw: ${seen}.`,
  )
}
```

```
#!/usr/bin/env bash
#
# `1c` launcher — thin wrapper around the generate CLI.
#
# Dispatches to tools/generate/bin/1c.mjs so you can type `1c <cmd>` instead of
# `node tools/generate/bin/1c.mjs <cmd>`. The repo root is resolved from this
# script's own location, so the launcher works from any directory. The caller's
# working directory is deliberately preserved (no `cd`): the CLI roots Vite at
# the repo but resolves site/dist paths relative to CWD, so `1c` behaves the
# same as invoking node directly.
#
# Usage:
#   ./bin/1c list
#   ./bin/1c render <slug> [--source draft|latest|<revId>]
#
# To type `1c` from anywhere, add this repo's bin/ to PATH, e.g.:
#   export PATH="/Users/martin/Projects/1stcontact/bin:$PATH"
# or alias it:
#   alias 1c="/Users/martin/Projects/1stcontact/bin/1c"
#
set -euo pipefail

here="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
repo_root="$(cd "$here/.." && pwd)"

exec node "$repo_root/tools/generate/bin/1c.mjs" "$@"
```

```json
{"content":"/**\n * [[BUG-359]] — OLD JOB-CONTAINER IMAGES ARE PRUNED, IN THE REGISTRY AND LOCALLY.\n *\n * Every production deploy pushes a ~1.5 GB job image to the account's Cloudflare\n * registry, and every dev start on a new snapshot builds a ~1.5 GB\n * `cloudflare-dev/<class>:<tag>` image; nothing removed either. Two prunes:\n *\n *  - {@link pruneRegistryImages}, after a successful production deploy: keep the\n *    image now deployed and the one before it (a one-version rollback), delete\n *    the rest. A delete that fails warns; the deploy has already succeeded.\n *  - {@link pruneLocalJobImages}, after a dev start succeeds: keep the tags that\n *    start built, and never an image a running container uses; `docker rmi`\n *    (never `-f`) the rest.\n */\nimport { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process'\nimport fs from 'node:fs'\nimport path from 'node:path'\nimport { CLOUDFLARE_API, resolveAccountId } from './cloudflare-account'\n\n/** The Durable Object classes an app's `wrangler.toml` runs containers for (`[[…containers]]` `class_name`). */\nexport function containerClasses(appDir: string): string[] {\n  let toml: string\n  try {\n    toml = fs.readFileSync(path.join(appDir, 'wrangler.toml'), 'utf8')\n  } catch {\n    return []\n  }\n  const out = new Set<string>()\n  let inContainers = false\n  for (const raw of toml.split('\\n')) {\n    const line = raw.trim()\n    if (line.startsWith('[')) {\n      inContainers = /^\\[\\[(?:env\\.[^.\\]]+\\.)?containers\\]\\]$/.test(line)\n      continue\n    }\n    const m = inContainers ? /**
```
```
 * Start the dev environment and record what was started.
 *
 * A SERVICE THAT IS ALREADY ANSWERING IS NOT AN ERROR AND IS NOT RESTARTED,
 * following `1c filing`'s own reason: two of them would fight over one port and
 * the second would lose, so the useful thing to say is that the first is doing
  const lines: string[] = [outcome.deploy.line]
  for (const s of outcome.started)
    lines.push(`  started  ${s.name.padEnd(12)} ${s.port}  ${describePids(s)}  → ${s.log}`)
  for (const a of outcome.alreadyUp)
    lines.push(
      `  already  ${a.name.padEnd(12)} ${a.port}  something was answering, so it was left alone` +
        ` (\`1c ps\` says whose it is)`,
    )
  for (const f of outcome.failed) {
    // REFUSED AND FAILED ARE DIFFERENT WORDS ON PURPOSE (REQ-322): one says a
    // reason exists and where it is, the other says nobody knows yet.
    const label = f.kind === 'refused' ? 'REFUSED ' : 'FAILED  '
    lines.push(`  ${label} ${f.name.padEnd(12)} ${f.port}  ${f.detail}`)
  }
  const sections = [
    `${outcome.ok ? 'The dev environment is up.' : 'The dev environment did not come up.'}\n` +
      lines.join('\n'),
  ]
  // WHAT IS BEING SERVED, PER APP, BEFORE THE FOOTER ([[BUG-150]]). The report
  // above says which ports answered; it does not say what is answering on them,
  // and that is the question an operator lost an evening to. `snapshotSummary` is
  // REUSED rather than restated so that `1c dev serve` and this cannot disagree
  // about the same server.
  const banner = outcome.served.map(formatServed).filter((text) => text !== '')
  // A BLANK LINE BETWEEN THEM. Two banners of five lines each run together as
  // one block, and the question they answer is per-app.
  if (banner.length > 0) sections.push(banner.join('\n\n'))
  const stale = formatStale(outcome.stale)
  if (stale !== '') sections.push(stale)
  sections.push(
    `\`1c ps\` lists what is running; \`bin/dev down\` stops it. \`bin/dev restart\` restarts EVERY\n` +
      `service; \`bin/dev restart <service>\` restarts only the ones named — which is what an\n` +
      `env-file change needs, since it only takes effect on a process started after it.`,
  )
  return sections.join('\n\n')
}
```

<!-- xgd-tool name="Bash" id="toolu_01L7uRc4appGsqB2vep1MAT7" turn="cbe975aa50484cd6b17779ab094e8237" ts="2026-11-06T01:16:14.103782+00:00" -->

**Result**

```
 * One line on success; on failure, one short paragraph per service naming what
 * was found and where its log is.
 *
 * NO LIST OF ALTERNATIVE COMMANDS ([[BUG-342]]). The operator used to be told
 * `bin/dev restart`, `bin/dev up`, `bin/dev restart <service>` or `bin/dev reap`
 * depending on the failure, and had to know which applied. What they need from a
 * failure is the fact — which service, what it did, where it said why.
 */
export function formatRefresh(outcome: DevRefreshOutcome): string {
  if (outcome.ok) {
    const served = outcome.snapshots
      .map((snap) => `${snap.app} ${snap.commit} (deployed ${snap.deployedAt})`)
      .join(', ')
    return `Dev is up: ${outcome.services.join(', ')} serving ${served}${notCompared(outcome)}`
  }
  if (outcome.occupied.length > 0) {
    return (
      `Dev was NOT brought up, and nothing was stopped or started: a dev service port is held by\n` +
      `a process that is not this checkout's, and a deploy never touches another checkout's or\n` +
      `project's server:\n${outcome.occupied.map((l) => l.line).join('\n')}`
    )
  }
  const paragraphs = outcome.failures.map((f) => {
    const lines = [`  ${f.name} (port ${f.port}): ${f.found}.`]
    if (f.tail.length > 0) lines.push(`    last log lines:\n${f.tail.map((l) => `      ${l}`).join('\n')}`)
    if (f.log !== '') lines.push(`    log: ${f.log}`)
    return lines.join('\n')
  })
  return `Dev is NOT up — ${outcome.failures.length} service(s) are not serving the new snapshot:\n\n${paragraphs.join('\n\n')}${notCompared(outcome)}`
}
```

```
  1c fonts doc [--json] [--stdout]
    Project DOC-56's body from fonts/catalogue.json and write it into the system-KB document
    that declares fonts/catalogue.json as its source. Only OFL 1.1 and Apache 2.0 families
    appear — the document is read as permission. Grouped by category with Slab Serif, Symbols
    and Noto split out, then sub-headings of 22 families by usage, because KB chunking is
    heading-anchored and a retrieval should return a slate rather than one family.
    --stdout prints the body instead of writing the document.
  1c platform-route [--dry-run] [--json]
    Route every platform hostname (*.1stc.site) to public-site (REQ-394): checks the zone is
    in the account and active, adds a proxied wildcard record if none exists, and adds the
    *.1stc.site/* Worker route if missing. Additive and idempotent — re-running changes
    nothing and it never deletes or replaces a record or route. Needs CLOUDFLARE_DNS_TOKEN.
    Run it through bin/platform-route, which loads the operator's secrets file.

Structured-edit commands (REQ-11) — operate on draft/; support --json:
  1c status <slug>
  1c changes <slug> [--since <n>]

# The deployed Worker's name for an environment. Read out of wrangler.toml rather
# than assumed to equal the directory: `name` under [env.<name>] overrides the
# top-level one, and reporting the wrong name is how a deploy gets confirmed
# against a Worker nobody changed.
worker_name() {
  toml_value "$1/wrangler.toml" "env.$2" name
}

# THE TARGET TABLE ([[REQ-318]]) — given an environment, how do I ship to it.
#
# WHY A TABLE AND NOT A HARDCODED `npx wrangler deploy`. This script's header has
# always said *"ONE CODE PATH. `--dry-run` is a TARGET, not a different script"*,
# and it already had the concept; what it lacked was the mapping. Everything
# before the terminal verb — the hook directories, their order, `DEPLOY_ENV`, the
# capability report — is IDENTICAL whichever row is selected, which is what makes
# a local deploy a deploy rather than an imitation of one.
#
```

```
-updated_at: '2026-11-05T21:43:51.776844+00:00'
+updated_at: '2026-11-05T21:48:46.924619+00:00'
+
+## Implementation defaults (BUG-359 session, 2026-11-05; change any before coding starts)
+
+- **Where the memory figures are recorded:** two new `turn_log` columns in migration `0050`. `peak_memory_source` (`cgroup` or `process_tree`) says which method produced the turn's peak. `memory_limit_mb` is the instance type's limit the peak was compared with. That comparison is how production "only records" it.
+- **How the job knows to enforce:** the limit comes from the `[[containers]]` `instance_type` through a table of Cloudflare instance types (`lite` 256 MiB, `basic` 1 GiB, `standard-1` 4 GiB, `standard-2` 6 GiB, `standard-3` 8 GiB, `standard-4` 12 GiB). A var, `JOB_MEMORY_ENFORCE=1`, is set in `[env.dev.vars]` and by the UAT rig, and absent in production. With it set, a step whose peak goes over t
+- **Registry pruning failures:** a delete that fails warns and leaves the deploy successful. The images go through the Cloudflare containers images API with the deploy's existing token.
+- **Which containers belong to a suite run:** a container named `workerd-<worker>-AgentContainer-*`, proxy sidecars included, created after the rig started. The rig removes those, and its stop fails if any remain.
+- **Local image pruning:** the images to keep are the tags that appeared during this dev start (wrangler gives each start a new random tag). The older tags are removed with a plain `docker rmi`, which Docker refuses for an image a container uses. *Accepted risk:* a dev deploy during a container test run can remove that run's image between two of its jobs.
== adeb43fcd3
-updated_at: '2026-11-05T21:41:07.686563+00:00'
+updated_at: '2026-11-05T21:43:51.776844+00:00'
+## Design decisions (agreed with the operator, 2026-11-05)
+
+1. **One ticket for all container clean-up, production and dev.** The operator chose to fold the production gaps into this ticket instead of filing a separate production bug. Severity is high because of the production cost and the live-token exposure.
+2. **The backstop is an alarm on the container's Durable Object, timed from idleness.**
+   - *Why on the DO:* the DO addresses the job's container and holds its token, so it's the one place that can stop both whatever happened to the Workflow. A Workflow-side `finally` can't help when the Workflow itself is killed.
+   - *Why idleness, not the step timeout:* a round step may run up to 2 hours (`ROUND_STEP.timeout`). An alarm set to "longest step + margin" would leave an abandoned container billing for 2 hours. Between steps the Workflow calls again within about a second (retry delay 1 s), so a short idle wait is safe.
+   - *`IDLE_STOP` = 5 minutes:* long enough to cover the Workflow's retry gaps with a wide margin, short enough that a leaked container costs pennies. It's one named constant, so it can be tuned.
+   - *A step still in flight* is left alone until the step timeout, after which the Workflow has given up on it anyway.
+   - *Rejected:* a `sleepAfter`-style idle stop from the `Container` helper class. The DO drives the raw `ctx.container` API, and the alarm gives the same result without swapping the class out.
+3. **`end` always stops the container,** even when its answer is an error. A retry of `end` then starts a fresh container to finish the job's bookkeeping. That's cheap, and it's correct because containers hold no state (architecture item 32).
+4. **Registry pruning keeps the deployed image plus the one before it.** That allows a one-version rollback. *Accepted trade-off:* rolling a Worker back further than one version loses its image and needs a redeploy. Pruning runs only after a successful production deploy. Dev runs locally and has no registry.
+5. **Dev doesn't try to cap the local container's memory.** Workerd creates local containers itself, with no memory limit, and wrangler 4.111 / miniflare have no option for one (checked). A `docker update --memory` after start was rejected as racy and hacky. Instead the job is told its instance type's limit (derived from `instance_type`, never hard-coded) and compares its whole-container peak wit
+6. **Peak memory is whole-container, never the Node process's `maxRSS`.** The preferred source is the cgroup's peak. Cloudflare runs containers in a VM, where the cgroup may not cover just the container. In that case the fallback is the summed RSS of the server's whole process tree from `/proc`, sampled about once a second. Falling back to `maxRSS` would bring back the very bug this fixes. The re
+7. **Local clean-up is part of the tooling, not a manual chore.** The container UAT suites tear down everything they started, sidecar proxies included, whether they pass, fail or time out. Dev start removes that app's older local images and never touches an image a running container uses. Already out of scope: `-production:worker` images (stopped by BUG-352) and one-off REQ-646 experiment images 
+
{+sessi
```

```
 tests/support/job-container-rig.ts         |  70 ++++++++++++++--
 tools/generate/src/cli/dev.ts              |  42 +++++++++-
```
