diff --git a/bin/deploy b/bin/deploy
index 422292e742..cd962e2b04 100755
--- a/bin/deploy
+++ b/bin/deploy
@@ -629,6 +629,24 @@ for app in "${targets[@]}"; do
   deployed+=("$name")
 done
 
+# [[BUG-359]] — OLD JOB IMAGES OUT OF THE REGISTRY, after the cloud deploy has
+# succeeded: every deploy of a container app pushes a ~1.5 GB image and nothing
+# else ever deletes one. Each app keeps the image its container application now
+# runs and the one before it (a one-version rollback). A failed delete warns and
+# never fails the deploy, which by now has shipped. `DEPLOY_PRUNE_IMAGES_CMD`
+# replaces the command (handed `--app <app> --worker <name>`), for the same reason
+# as `DEPLOY_BUILD_CMD`. A rehearsal and a local deploy push nothing to prune.
+if [[ $dry_run -eq 0 && "$target" != "local" ]]; then
+  step "Prune old job images from the registry"
+  prune_cmd="${DEPLOY_PRUNE_IMAGES_CMD:-$repo_root/bin/1c images prune}"
+  for app in "${targets[@]}"; do
+    app_dir="$repo_root/apps/$app"
+    name="$(worker_name "$app_dir" "$env_name")"
+    [[ -n "$name" ]] || name="$app"
+    $prune_cmd --app "$app" --worker "$name" || echo "  ⚠ $app: the image prune did not run; the deploy is unaffected"
+  done
+fi
+
 # THE REPORT COMES BEFORE THE DEPLOYED LIST AND AFTER THE UPLOAD, deliberately:
 # it describes what the thing just uploaded can and cannot do, which is only
 # answerable once every hook has run. A deploy that stopped at a hook never
diff --git a/tools/generate/src/cli/index.ts b/tools/generate/src/cli/index.ts
index 73a60d86ed..789033691f 100644
--- a/tools/generate/src/cli/index.ts
+++ b/tools/generate/src/cli/index.ts
@@ -960,6 +960,11 @@ Fonts (REQ-101) — licence provenance for every font file in the project:
     and Noto split out, then sub-headings of 22 families by usage, because KB chunking is
     heading-anchored and a retrieval should return a slate rather than one family.
     --stdout prints the body instead of writing the document.
+  1c images prune --app <app> --worker <name> [--json]
+    BUG-359: delete the app's older job-container images from the Cloudflare registry, keeping
+    the image its container application now runs and the one before it. bin/deploy runs it
+    after a successful cloud deploy; a delete that fails warns and never fails the deploy.
+    Needs CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN (CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID when the token sees several accounts).
   1c platform-route [--dry-run] [--json]
     Route every platform hostname (*.1stc.site) to public-site (REQ-394): checks the zone is
     in the account and active, adds a proxied wildcard record if none exists, and adds the
@@ -2983,6 +2988,36 @@ export async function run(argv: string[]): Promise<void> {
      * route attached at runtime. Loaded lazily so no other command pays for the
      * control app's modules.
      */
+    /**
+     * `1c images prune` — the app's older job images, out of the registry ([[BUG-359]]).
+     * Always exits 0 once it has run: the deploy before it has already succeeded.
+     */
+    case 'images': {
+      const json = flags.json === true
+      try {
+        if (rest[0] !== 'prune') throw new Error('usage: 1c images prune --app <app> --worker <name>')
+        const app = typeof flags.app === 'string' ? flags.app : ''
+        const worker = typeof flags.worker === 'string' ? flags.worker : ''
+        if (app === '' || worker === '') throw new Error('1c images prune needs --app and --worker')
+        const { containerClasses, pruneRegistryImages, formatRegistryPrune } = await import('./container-images')
+        const classes = containerClasses(path.join(repoRoot(), 'apps', app))
+        const token = (process.env.CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN ?? '').trim()
+        if (classes.length === 0) {
+          if (json) console.log(JSON.stringify({ ok: true, data: [] }, null, 2))
+          else console.log(`  ${app} runs no containers; nothing to prune`)
+          return
+        }
+        const results = token
+          ? await pruneRegistryImages({ token, worker, classes })
+          : classes.map((c) => ({ application: `${worker}-${c.toLowerCase()}`, repository: null, kept: [], deleted: [], failed: [], skipped: 'CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN is not set' }))
+        if (json) console.log(JSON.stringify({ ok: true, data: results }, null, 2))
+        else console.log(formatRegistryPrune(results))
+      } catch (err) {
+        fail(err, json)
+      }
+      return
+    }
+

Worktree and shared-store setup. Each entry is its own memory:

- [[pnpm-install-blocked-by-idea-dir]] — pnpm install blocked by .idea dir: sandbox denies `.idea`; iconv-lite ships one.
- [[worktree-breaks-webui-resolution]] — Worktree breaks webui resolution: builder/webui 404s and workerd import death are location artifacts.
- [[shared-node-modules-outside-sandbox]] — Shared node_modules outside sandbox: `bin/install` can't write the shared store.
- [[pnpm-store-closure-from-main-checkout]] — pnpm store closure from main checkout: a missing declared package needs no network.
- [[unpinned-shared-store-drifts]] — Unpinned shared store drifts: `@lagrangefoundry/*` isn't in the lockfile.
- [[reference-bundles-are-gitignored]] — Reference bundles are gitignored: real-bundle UATs skip in every worktree.
- [[shadow-shared-store-to-verify-upstream]] — Shadow the shared store: stage into 1stcontact/node_modules, then delete.
- [[repair-stale-worktree-install-by-hand]] — Repair a stale worktree install by hand: worktree sits at the branch's lockfile.
- [[baseline-real-bundle-tests-in-place]] — Baseline real-bundle tests in place: revert your files in the main checkout instead.
- [[fresh-worktree-lacks-control-app-generated]] — Fresh worktree lacks control-app generated: "0 test" is a missing artifact.
- [[patch-swap-baseline-needs-exclude]] — Patch-swap baseline needs --exclude: git apply is atomic; module-assets/tests hunks make it silently refuse.

**Result**

```
---
name: job-container-uat-rig
description: "Running REQ-646's container UATs in a worktree: dist-assets symlink, fresh src/generated after merging, one test per call."
metadata:
---

`tests/test_UAT_FC_REQ-646_agent_compute_in_containers.test.ts` runs the control app under `unstable_dev` with real job containers (Docker).
- **A worktree needs `apps/control-app/dist-assets`**, or `unstable_dev` refuses the config. Symlink the main checkout's copy, and never stage the symlink.
- **Re-copy `apps/control-app/src/generated` after merging xgd-working into a branch.** The stale copy made BUG-322's stage-guide UAT fail on the branch only, which looked like a regression.
- **Run one case per Bash call** (`-t <name>`): each takes 1–8 min (the 400-product/2,000-item case is about 500 s), so the whole file overruns 590 s.
- `getPlatformProxy` on the full `wrangler.toml` fails because the AI binding is `remote = true`. The rig seeds through a D1/R2-only config whose persist path is `<persistTo>/v3`.

Related: [[docker-builds-need-temp-docker-config]], [[worktree-and-store-setup]], [[full-suite-in-foreground-batches]].
---
name: branch-cut-blocked-by-hookspath
description: "xgd branch cut fails in the sandbox setting core.hooksPath, after already creating the branch; attach the worktree by hand."
metadata:
---

`xgd branch cut <ID>` in a sandboxed session dies with *"cannot set core.hooksPath … could not lock config file .git/config: Operation not permitted"* — but only AFTER it created `free-<ID>` at xgd-working's tip. Seen 2026-11-02 (BUG-329).

**How to apply:** `git worktree add ~/.xgd/worktrees/git_github.com_lagrangefoundry_1stcontact.git/free-<ID> free-<ID>` works (no `-b`; the branch exists). Then make it runnable: `node_modules` as a real dir of per-entry symlinks to the main checkout's (`ls -A $M/node_modules | xargs -I{} ln -s …`) plus `@lagrangefoundry` → `/Users/martin/lagrangefoundry/node_modules/@lagrangefoundry`; symlink each package's `node_modules` (`apps/control-app`, `tools/generate`, `tools/repro-console`, `packages/site-schema`, `packages/framework`, `apps/public-site`) individually (xargs with `sh -c` overflowed); copy `apps/control-app/src/generated`. Without the package links, plan-panel suites die at collection on `@1stcontact/site-schema`.

**Never run `pnpm <script>` in a worktree built this way (BUG-333, 2026-11-03).** pnpm 11 checks dependencies before a run and rewrites every package's `node_modules` links and `.bin` shims. Through the symlinked package dirs, that rewrites the MAIN checkout's links to point into the worktree. After `xgd branch clean` they dangle, and main's `1c` dies with "Cannot find package 'vite'", which breaks `bin/deploy`.
- **Repair:** re-point each link (`find … -lname '*free-<ID>*'`) to the same repo-relative path in the main checkout, then `sed` the worktree path out of the `.bin` shims' `NODE_PATH`.
- **Safer:** run `node_modules/.bin/vitest …` directly, or give a worktree that must run `pnpm` its own real `node_modules`.
- **For `pnpm test:browser` (has to be `pnpm`, for the sandbox exclusion to apply):** append `verifyDepsBeforeRun: false` to the worktree's `pnpm-workspace.yaml` for the run, then `git checkout pnpm-workspace.yaml`. Main's links stayed intact (REQ-639, 2026-11-03).

**Don't symlink a package's whole `node_modules` (REQ-643, 2026-11-04).** `tools/generate/node_modules/@1stcontact/framework` is a RELATIVE link (`../../../../packages/framework`), resolved from where it really lives. Through a symlinked dir, that is the MAIN checkout's `packages/`, so typecheck and tests run main's framework, not the branch's. Make each package's `node_modules` a real dir: symlink every entry except `@1stcontact`, and recreate `@1stcontact/*` as the same relative links inside the worktree.

`xgd-merge` releases the lock on exit, so another ticket can grab it before `xgd branch clean` runs → [[branch-clean-blocked-by-another-lock]]. Related: [[worktree-and-store-setup]], [[xgd-free-coded-promotion]].
---
name: fresh-worktree-lacks-control-app-generated
description: Five-plus node suites fail in a freshly cut worktree because apps/control-app/src/generated/ is a build artifact that only exists in the main checkout.
metadata: 
---

`apps/control-app/src/generated/` (ai-workers.js, knowledge, ticketing, logging, importmap.json, …) is a **build artifact**, present in `/Users/martin/lagrangefoundry/1stcontact` and absent in a freshly cut `xgd branch cut` worktree.

Any test that transitively imports control-app therefore dies at collection with `Cannot find module './generated/knowledge'` and reports **"0 test"** — including `reconciliation-l1-authoring-envelope`, `reconciliation-l1-fold-full-language`, `bug15-values-diff-l1-flat-dom`, `reconciliation-builder-request-time-render`, `test_UAT_FC_REQ-130_beyond_l1`, `req118-image-selection`. A CLI-subprocess test in the same set (`reconciliation-1c-astro-free-render`, asserting `res.status === 0`) fails for the same reason but presents as a real assertion failure, not an import error.

**Why:** reads as "my change broke six suites" when it is purely a location artifact — same class as [[worktree-breaks-webui-resolution]] and [[repair-stale-worktree-install-by-hand]].

**How to apply:** before diagnosing, `ls apps/control-app/src/generated/`. Empty → `cp -R /Users/martin/lagrangefoundry/1stcontact/apps/control-app/src/generated apps/control-app/src/`. It is gitignored, so nothing gets committed. That made the `.workers` delegation suites run in a worktree on 2026-10-18 (BUG-225). Do not report the import failures as regressions.

**And it does not stay right (REQ-624, 2026-11-01):** a suite that runs `1c assets` rewrites the worktree's `generated/kb.js` to `export const KB = null`, so 
```

[elided — kept 5800 of 6055 bytes]

```

**Result**

```
#!/bin/sh
# xgd release shim (REQ-934). A pinned run stays on its release; everything
# else follows the newest deployed release.
root="$HOME/.xgd/releases"
if [ -n "$XGD_RUN_RELEASE" ]; then
  case "$XGD_RUN_RELEASE" in
    dev:*)
      if [ -x "$XGD_RUN_PYTHON" ]; then exec "$XGD_RUN_PYTHON" -m xgd_source "$@"; fi
      echo "xgd: pinned interpreter $XGD_RUN_PYTHON is missing" >&2
      exit 3
      ;;
  esac
  target="$root/$XGD_RUN_RELEASE/bin/xgd"
  if [ ! -x "$target" ]; then
    echo "xgd: pinned release $XGD_RUN_RELEASE is missing ($target)" >&2
    exit 3
  fi
else
  target="$root/current/bin/xgd"
fi
exec "$target" "$@"
```

diff --git a/apps/control-app/src/agent-container-do.ts b/apps/control-app/src/agent-container-do.ts
index f8b9b47d25..faadf0784b 100644
--- a/apps/control-app/src/agent-container-do.ts
+++ b/apps/control-app/src/agent-container-do.ts
@@ -1,5 +1,5 @@
 import { DurableObject } from 'cloudflare:workers'
-import { JobContainer, type ContainerLike, type ContainerStepResult } from './agent-container'
+import { JobContainer, type ContainerLike, type ContainerStepResult, type TokenStorage } from './agent-container'
 import type { StepRequest } from './job-step'
 
 /**
@@ -12,7 +12,8 @@ import type { StepRequest } from './job-step'
  * this only hands it the platform's container, this object's storage and the
  * `PLATFORM` binding the container's egress is sent to.
  *
- * IT HOLDS NO STATE BUT THE JOB'S TOKEN, and no compute: a Durable Object shares
+ * IT HOLDS NO STATE BUT THE JOB'S TOKEN AND ITS STEP CLOCK ([[BUG-359]]'s stop
+ * backstop, an alarm on this object), and no compute: a Durable Object shares
  * its isolate's 128 MB with others ([[DOC-1]] item 7). The work is the container's.
  */
 export class AgentContainer extends DurableObject<Record<string, unknown>> {
@@ -21,7 +22,7 @@ export class AgentContainer extends DurableObject<Record<string, unknown>> {
   constructor(ctx: DurableObjectState, env: Record<string, unknown>) {
     super(ctx, env)
     if (!ctx.container) throw new Error('AgentContainer is bound without a [[containers]] entry')
-    this.job = new JobContainer(ctx.container as unknown as ContainerLike, ctx.storage, env, env.PLATFORM)
+    this.job = new JobContainer(ctx.container as unknown as ContainerLike, ctx.storage as unknown as TokenStorage, env, env.PLATFORM)
   }
 
   /** Run one step of the job in its container. */
@@ -29,6 +30,11 @@ export class AgentContainer extends DurableObject<Record<string, unknown>> {
     return this.job.runStep(request)
   }
 
+  /** [[BUG-359]] — the stop backstop: a job whose Workflow went quiet loses its container and token. */
+  alarm(): Promise<void> {
+    return this.job.alarm()
+  }
+
   /** Whether `token` is this job's token: asked by `PlatformApi` on every call. */

import path from 'node:path'
import { afterAll, beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'
import { backendsDocument } from '../tools/generate/src/cli/ai/backends'
import { ticketStoreFor } from '../apps/control-app/src/tickets'

 * Every case drives the control app as `wrangler dev` runs it for local

 * `AgentContainer` Durable Object and the job's real container, built from

 * message and reads the stream, exactly as the builder's chat does. The model is
 * the one double, served over HTTP to the container (`model-server.ts`).
 *
 * The container is killed with `docker kill` (a crash, or a memory kill) and
 * stopped with `docker stop` (SIGTERM, a deploy) — what the platform does to a

})

const write = (section: string, text: string): ModelStep =>
  calls('set_l1', { page: 'home', section, path: '0', node: { kind: 'text', id: section, text, axes: { fontSizePx: 16 } } })

const report = (summary: string): ModelStep => calls('ReportResult', { summary, changed: [], decisions: [], passed: [], failed: [] })

const spoken = (seen: Frame[]): string => seen.filter((f) => f.kind === 'text').map((f) => f.content).join('')

/** Who the model is asked as, and the script each answers from. */
interface Script {
  consultant: ModelStep[]
  builder: (req: ModelRequest, n: number) => Promise<ModelStep | null> | ModelStep | null
}

let script: Script = { consultant: [says('Hello.')], builder: () => report('Nothing.') }
const asked = { consultant: [] as ModelRequest[], builder: [] as ModelRequest[] }

function play(next: Script): void {
  script = next
  asked.consultant.length = 0
  asked.builder.length = 0
}

const until = async (ok: () => Promise<boolean> | boolean, ms = 120_000): Promise<void> => {
  const end = Date.now() + ms
  while (!(await ok())) {
    if (Date.now() > end) throw new Error('timed out waiting')
    await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 250))
  }
}

/** Worker secrets that must never reach a container (the Worker has them for its own calls). */
const SECRETS = {
  CLOUDFLARE_DNS_TOKEN: 'dns-token-must-not-travel',
  RESEND_API_KEY: 'resend-key-must-not-travel',
  DEVELOPMENT_TICKETS_TOKEN: 'tickets-token-must-not-travel',

}

export interface ServerCompute {
  peakMemoryMb: number | null
  cpuS: number | null
  peakMemorySource?: PeakMemorySource | null
  memoryLimitMb?: number | null
}

export type ContainerStepResult =
  | { ok: true; answer: StepAnswer; compute: StepCompute }
  | { ok: false; message: string; compute: StepCompute }

export interface ContainerLike {
  readonly running: boolean
  start(options: { enableInternet: boolean; env: Record<string, string> }): void
  monitor(): Promise<void>
  destroy(error?: unknown): Promise<void>
  getTcpPort(port: number): { fetch(input: string, init?: RequestInit): Promise<Response> }
  interceptOutboundHttp?(addr: string, binding: unknown): Promise<void>
}

export interface TokenStorage {
  get<T>(key: string): Promise<T | undefined>
  put(key: string, value: unknown): Promise<void>
  delete(key: string): Promise<boolean>
  setAlarm(scheduledTime: number): Promise<void>
  deleteAlarm(): Promise<void>
}

export const IDLE_STOP_MS = 5 * 60 * 1000

export const INFLIGHT_STOP_MS = STEP_TIMEOUT_MS + 60_000

interface StepClock {
  inflightSince: number | null
  idleSince: number
}

const READY_WITHIN_MS = 90_000

const LIVENESS_EVERY_MS = 2_000

const EXIT_WITHIN_MS = 10_000

export class JobContainer {
  private exited: Promise<ContainerExit> | null = null

  constructor(
    private readonly container: ContainerLike,
    private readonly storage: TokenStorage,
    private readonly env: Record<string, unknown>,
    private readonly platform: unknown,
    private readonly now: () => number = () => Date.now(),
  ) {}

  async verify(token: string): Promise<boolean> {
    const held = await this.storage.get<string>('token')
    return typeof held === 'string' && sameToken(held, token)
  }

  private async token(): Promise<string> {
    const held = await this.storage.get<string>('token')
    if (typeof held === 'string' && held !== '') return held
    const fresh = mintToken()
    await this.storage.put('token', fresh)
    return fresh
  }

  private async ready(job: string): Promise<void> {
    if (!this.container.running) {
      this.container.start({ enableInternet: true, env: containerEnv(this.env, job, await this.token()) })
      this.exited = this.container.monitor().then(
        () => ({ exitCode: 0 }),
        (err: unknown) => exitOf(err),
      )
      await this.container.interceptOutboundHttp?.(PLATFORM_ADDR, this.platform)
    }
    const port = this.container.getTcpPort(CONTAINER_PORT)
    const deadline = this.now() + READY_WITHIN_MS
    for (;;) {
      try {
        if ((await port.fetch('http://container/health')).ok) return
      } catch {
        // Not listening yet.
      }
      if (this.now() > deadline) throw new Error('the job\'s container did not start')
      await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 250))
    }
      if (this.container.running) await this.container.destroy().catch(() => {})
      this.exited = null
      return { ok: false, message: 'an earlier attempt was still running; its container was stopped', compute: compute('crash') }
    }
    if (body?.checkpoint) {
      return { ok: false, message: 'the job\'s container was stopped; the step checkpointed', compute: compute('sigterm', body.compute ?? null) }
    }
    return { ok: false, message: body?.error ?? `the job's container answered ${response.status}`, compute: compute('error', body?.compute ?? null) }
  }

  /** Rejects once the container has stopped: its exit reported, or it no longer running. */
  private stopped(): { promise: Promise<never>; cancel(): void } {
    let timer: ReturnType<typeof setInterval> | null = null
    let cancelled = false
    const promise = new Promise<never>((_, reject) => {
      const gone = () => reject(new Error('the container exited'))
      void this.exited?.then(() => {
        if (!cancelled) gone()
      })
      timer = setInterval(() => {
        if (!cancelled && !this.container.running) gone()
      }, LIVENESS_EVERY_MS)
    })
    promise.catch(() => {})
    return {
      promise,
      cancel: () => {
        cancelled = true
        if (timer !== null) clearInterval(timer)
      },
    }

```
/**
 * [[BUG-359]] — WHAT THE WHOLE JOB CONTAINER USES, AND WHAT IT IS ALLOWED.
 *
 * The step's peak memory used to be the Node server's own `maxRSS`, which leaves
 * out Chromium: Chromium runs as child processes and counts against the instance's
 * limit all the same (the live container used 1.7 GB while its row said 854 MB).
 * So the peak is now the container's:
 *
 *  - `cgroup` — the container's own memory cgroup, where there is one: its usage
 *    less the inactive file cache the kernel reclaims before it kills anything
 *    (what `docker stats` reports). A root cgroup has no `memory.current`, so a
 *    cgroup that would cover the whole machine (a VM's) is never read as this.
 *  - `process_tree` — otherwise, the summed resident set of the server's whole
 *    process tree, from `/proc` (or `ps` where there is no `/proc`).
 *
 * NEVER THE NODE PROCESS'S OWN `maxRSS`, which is the bug. The figure says which
 * of the two produced it.
 *
 * Sampled about once a second while a step runs, because neither source keeps a
 * per-step peak: `memory.peak` is the cgroup's lifetime high-water mark, and a
 * process tree has none.
 */
import { execFile } from 'node:child_process'
import { existsSync } from 'node:fs'
import { readFile, readdir } from 'node:fs/promises'
import path from 'node:path'

export type PeakMemorySource = 'cgroup' | 'process_tree'

/** One way of reading the container's memory now, MB. */
export interface MemoryProbe {
  readonly source: PeakMemorySource
  /** The memory in use now, MB; `null` when it could not be read this time. */
  read(): Promise<number | null>
}

const MB = 1024 * 1024

/** The usage, and the stat whose `inactive_file` is reclaimable, of a cgroup v2 or v1 hierarchy. */
function cgroupFiles(root: string): { usage: string; stat: string; inactive: string } | null {
  const v2 = path.join(root, 'memory.current')
  if (existsSync(v2)) return { usage: v2, stat: path.join(root, 'memory.stat'), inactive: 'inactive_file' }
  const v1 = path.join(root, 'memory', 'memory.usage_in_bytes')
  if (existsSync(v1)) return { usage: v1, stat: path.join(root, 'memory', 'memory.stat'), inactive: 'total_inactive_file' }
  return null
}

/** The container's cgroup, where one covers just the container; else `null`. */
export function cgroupProbe(root = '/sys/fs/cgroup'): MemoryProbe | null {
  const files = cgroupFiles(root)
  if (!files) return null
  return {
    source: 'cgroup',
    read: async () => {
      try {
        const usage = Number((await readFile(files.usage, 'utf8')).trim())
        if (!Number.isFinite(usage)) return null
        const stat = await readFile(files.stat, 'utf8').catch(() => '')
        const line = stat.split('\n').find((l) => l.startsWith(`${files.inactive} `))
        const inactive = line ? Number(line.split(' ')[1]) : 0
        return Math.max(0, usage - (Number.isFinite(inactive) ? inactive : 0)) / MB
      } catch {
        return null
      }
    },
  }
}

/** pid → [parent pid, resident KiB], for every process this one can see. */
type ProcessTable = Map<number, [number, number]>

async function procTable(): Promise<ProcessTable> {
  const table: ProcessTable = new Map()
  for (const entry of await readdir('/proc')) {
    if (!/^\d+$/.test(entry)) continue
    try {
      const status = await readFile(`/proc/${entry}/status`, 'utf8')
      const ppid = /^PPid:\s+(\d+)/m.exec(status)
      const rss = /^VmRSS:\s+(\d+)\s*kB/m.exec(status)
      table.set(Number(entry), [ppid ? Number(ppid[1]) : 0, rss ? Number(rss[1]) : 0])
    } catch {
      // A process that ended between the listing and the read.
    }
  }
  return table
}

function psTable(): Promise<ProcessTable> {
  return new Promise((resolve) => {
    execFile('ps', ['-A', '-o', 'pid=,ppid=,rss='], { maxBuffer: 16 * MB }, (err, stdout) => {
      const table: ProcessTable = new Map()
      if (!err) {
        for (const line of stdout.split('\n')) {
          const [pid, ppid, rss] = line.trim().split(/\s+/).map(Number)
          if (Number.isFinite(pid) && Number.isFinite(ppid) && Number.isFinite(rss)) table.set(pid!, [ppid!, rss!])
        }
      }
      resolve(table)
    })
  })
}

/** The summed resident set of `root` and every process descended from it, MB. */
export function treeRssMb(table: ProcessTable, root: number): number | null {
  if (!table.has(root)) return null
  const children = new Map<number, number[]>()
  for (const [pid, [ppid]] of table) {
    if (pid === root) continue
    const list = children.get(ppid) ?? []
    list.push(pid)
    children.set(ppid, list)
  }
  let kib = 0
  const queue = [root]
  const seen = new Set<number>()
  while (queue.length > 0) {
    const pid = queue.pop()!
    if (seen.has(pid)) continue
    seen.add(pid)
    kib += table.get(pid)?.[1] ?? 0
    queue.push(...(children.get(pid) ?? []))
  }
  return kib / 1024
}

/** The summed resident set of the process tree rooted at `root` (this process by default). */
export function processTreeProbe(root = process.pid): MemoryProbe {
  const proc = existsSync('/proc/self/status')
  return {
    source: 'process_tree',
    read: async () => treeRssMb(await (proc ? procTable() : psTable()), root),
  }
}

/** The container's cgroup where one covers just it, else its process tree. */
export function containerMemoryProbe(): MemoryProbe {
  return cgroupProbe() ?? processTreeProbe()
}

/**
 * [[BUG-359]] — OLD JOB-CONTAINER IMAGES ARE PRUNED, IN THE REGISTRY AND LOCALLY.
 *
 * Every production deploy pushes a ~1.5 GB job image to the account's Cloudflare
 * registry, and every dev start on a new snapshot builds a ~1.5 GB
 * `cloudflare-dev/<class>:<tag>` image; nothing removed either. Two prunes:
 *
 *  - {@link pruneRegistryImages}, after a successful production deploy: keep the
 *    image now deployed and the one before it (a one-version rollback), delete
 *    the rest. A delete that fails warns; the deploy has already succeeded.
 *  - {@link pruneLocalJobImages}, after a dev start succeeds: keep the tags that
 *    start built, and never an image a running container uses; `docker rmi`
 *    (never `-f`) the rest.
 */
import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process'
import fs from 'node:fs'
import path from 'node:path'
import { CLOUDFLARE_API, resolveAccountId } from './cloudflare-account'

/** The Durable Object classes an app's `wrangler.toml` runs containers for (`[[…containers]]` `class_name`). */
export function containerClasses(appDir: string): string[] {
  let toml: string
  try {
    toml = fs.readFileSync(path.join(appDir, 'wrangler.toml'), 'utf8')
  } catch {
    return []
  }
  const out = new Set<string>()
  let inContainers = false
  for (const raw of toml.split('\n')) {
    const line = raw.trim()
    if (line.startsWith('[')) {
      inContainers = /^\[\[(?:env\.[^.\]]+\.)?containers\]\]$/.test(line)
      continue
    }
    const m = inContainers ? /^class_name\s*=\s*"([^"]+)"/.exec(line) : null
    if (m) out.add(m[1]!)
  }
  return [...out]
}

// ─── The registry ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

const MANIFEST_ACCEPT = 'application/vnd.oci.image.manifest.v1+json, application/vnd.docker.distribution.manifest.v2+json'

export interface RegistryPruneOptions {
  /** The deploy's own `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`. */
  token: string
  /** The Worker just deployed (`name` of the environment deployed). */
  worker: string
  /** Its container classes ({@link containerClasses}). */
  classes: readonly string[]
  /** `process.env` by default: `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`, `CLOUDFLARE_CONTAINER_REGISTRY`. */
  env?: Readonly<Record<string, string | undefined>>
  fetch?: typeof fetch
}

/** What the prune did for one container application's repository. */
export interface RegistryPruneResult {
  application: string
  /** The repository, without the account prefix. */
  repository: string | null
  /** The tags kept: the deployed one first, then the one before it. */
  kept: string[]
  deleted: string[]
  /** Deletes that failed, each with why. */
  failed: { tag: string; error: string }[]
  /** Why nothing was pruned, when nothing could safely be. */
  skipped: string | null
}

/**
 * [[BUG-359]] — delete the Worker's older job images from the Cloudflare registry,

 * WHICH IMAGE IS DEPLOYED is the application's own configuration, not a guess
 * from the tags. Wrangler tags each image it pushes with the Worker version's id
 * (its first segment), so the tags are ordered by their versions' upload times;
 * a tag no version accounts for is older than every one that is.
 */
export async function pruneRegistryImages(opts: RegistryPruneOptions): Promise<RegistryPruneResult[]> {
  const env = opts.env ?? process.env

**Result**

```
/**
 * [[BUG-203]] — `bin/deploy` shipped stale builder browser assets beside a fresh
 * Worker.
 *
 * WHAT WAS WRONG. The deploy COPIED `apps/control-app/dist-assets` and never
 * built it; only `bin/build` / `1c assets` did. The Worker bundle, though, is
 * rebuilt from source on every deploy — so a deploy without a fresh build
 * shipped today's server beside yesterday's builder client and said nothing. On
 * 2026-10-04 the comp board was live on the server and absent from the browser.
 *
 * WHAT CHANGED. A `bin/deploy.d/assets/` hook stage runs FIRST, before migrate
 * and secrets, at every target. Its control-app hook runs `1c assets`, so what
 * the deploy ships (or freezes into the dev snapshot) is what it just built.
 *
 * WHAT THESE PIN:
 *   1. A builder file whose `dist-assets` copy is stale is rebuilt by the hook,
 *      byte-for-byte to the source — whatever directory the hook is run from.
 *   2. `bin/deploy` runs the build stage before every other hook, at the local
 *      AND the cloud target.
 *   3. A build that fails stops the deploy: no migrate hook, nothing deployed.
 *   4. A rehearsal builds nothing and says what it would build.
 *
 * NOT A FULL `bin/deploy --env dev control-app`. That runs the control app's
 * migrate hook against `.wrangler/state`, which in the main checkout is the only
 * copy of the dev data. The chain is proved in its parts instead: the hook makes
 * `dist-assets` match the source (1), the deploy runs it before the ship step
 * (2, 3), and the ship step copies `dist-assets` into the snapshot (REQ-318).
 */
import { describe, it, expect } from 'vitest'
import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process'
import {
  chmodSync,
  existsSync,
  mkdirSync,
  readFileSync,
  readdirSync,
  rmSync,
  utimesSync,
  writeFileSync,
} from 'node:fs'
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os'
import path from 'node:path'

const REPO = path.resolve(import.meta.dirname, '..')
const DEPLOY = path.join(REPO, 'bin', 'deploy')
const HOOK = path.join(REPO, 'bin', 'deploy.d', 'assets', '10-control-app-assets')
const CONTROL_APP = path.join(REPO, 'apps', 'control-app')
const SOURCE = path.join(CONTROL_APP, 'src', 'builder')
const BUILT = path.join(CONTROL_APP, 'dist-assets', 'builder')
const PROBE = 'plan-panel.js'
// `1c assets` regenerates this committed file; a test must not leave it dirty.
const MODULE_ASSETS = path.join(REPO, 'packages', 'framework', 'src', 'modules', 'module-assets.ts')

function run(file: string, args: string[], env: Record<string, string> = {}, cwd = REPO) {
  try {
    const out = execFileSync(file, args, {
      cwd,
      encoding: 'utf8',
      env: { ...process.env, ...env },
      stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'],
      timeout: 5 * 60_000,
    })
    return { ok: true, out }
  } catch (err) {
    const e = err as { stdout?: string; stderr?: string }
    return { ok: false, out: `${e.stdout ?? ''}${e.stderr ?? ''}` }
  }
}

function hookEnv(dry: '0' | '1'): Record<string, string> {
  return {
    DEPLOY_APP: 'control-app',
    DEPLOY_APP_DIR: CONTROL_APP,
    DEPLOY_ENV: 'dev',
    DEPLOY_TARGET: 'local',
    DEPLOY_DRY_RUN: dry,
    DEPLOY_REPO_ROOT: REPO,
  }
}

/** Every file under `dir`, relative to it. */
function walk(dir: string, rel = ''): string[] {
  return readdirSync(path.join(dir, rel), { withFileTypes: true }).flatMap((d) =>
    d.isDirectory() ? walk(dir, path.join(rel, d.name)) : [path.join(rel, d.name)],
  )
}

/** Make the built copy of the probe file stale: older bytes, older mtime. */
function staleCopy(): string {
  mkdirSync(BUILT, { recursive: true })
  const stale = `// bug203 stale copy ${Date.now()}\n`
  writeFileSync(path.join(BUILT, PROBE), stale)
  const yesterday = new Date(Date.now() - 86_400_000)
  utimesSync(path.join(BUILT, PROBE), yesterday, yesterday)
  return stale
}

/** Install temporary hooks for the duration of `fn`. */
function withHooks<T>(hooks: { kind: string; name: string; body: string }[], fn: () => T): T {
  const files = hooks.map((h) => path.join(REPO, 'bin', 'deploy.d', h.kind, h.name))
  hooks.forEach((h, i) => {
    writeFileSync(files[i], `#!/usr/bin/env bash\n${h.body}\n`)
    chmodSync(files[i], 0o755)
  })
  try {
    return fn()
  } finally {
    for (const f of files) rmSync(f, { force: true })
  }
}

# THE STORE IS GUARDED ON ENTRY ([[REQ-318]], [[REQ-316]]), and the placement is
# the whole of it: the FIRST thing a local deploy does is run the migrate hook
# against `.wrangler/state`, and that store is workerd's own — workerd migrates
# its schema forward silently and one-way the first time it opens it, so a tree
# with two runtimes in it loses data rather than reporting an error. This
# environment holds the only copy of the dev data (EPIC-16 §K2/§M1), so the check
# runs before any hook rather than as a diagnosis afterwards. The cloud target
# opens no local store and is not gated.
if [[ "$target" == "local" ]]; then
  "$repo_root/bin/1c" workerd --quiet
fi

if [[ $dry_run -eq 1 ]]; then
  echo "DRY RUN — hooks are told not to change anything, and nothing is uploaded."
  echo "Capability probes are reads, so they run anyway and report as usual."
fi

# THE FONTS, ONCE, BEFORE THE FIRST APP ([[REQ-412]]). Once and not per app
# because the cloud bucket is one bucket; at the local target the apps being
# deployed are passed so only their stores are checked. After the workerd guard,
# because a seed opens `.wrangler/state`. A changed mirror that is not fully
# staged exits non-zero here, under `set -e`, before any hook or upload.
step "platform fonts"
fonts_cmd=("$repo_root/bin/1c" fonts deploy --target "$target")
if [[ "$target" == "local" ]]; then
  fonts_cmd+=(--app "$(IFS=,; echo "${targets[*]}")")
fi
if [[ $no_fonts -eq 1 ]]; then
  fonts_cmd+=(--check)
  echo "  --no-fonts: checking only — nothing is delivered"
elif [[ $dry_run -eq 1 ]]; then
  fonts_cmd+=(--dry-run)
fi
DEPLOY_CAPABILITY_REPORT="$capability_report" "${fonts_cmd[@]}"

deployed=()
for app in "${targets[@]}"; do
  app_dir="$repo_root/apps/$app"
  name="$(worker_name "$app_dir" "$env_name")"
  [[ -n "$name" ]] || name="$app"

  step "$app → $name (--env $env_name, $target)"
  # BUILD FIRST ([[BUG-203]]): a deploy ships what it just built, never what an
  # earlier `bin/build` left lying in `dist-assets`, and a build that fails stops
  # the deploy before any migration is applied for code that will not ship.
  run_hooks assets "$app" "$app_dir" "$name"
  run_hooks migrate "$app" "$app_dir" "$name"
  run_hooks secrets "$app" "$app_dir" "$name"

  if [[ "$target" == "local" ]]; then
    ship_local "$app" "$app_dir" "$name"
  else
    # Composed once. --dry-run is appended to the SAME command line the real
    # deploy uses, so the two cannot drift apart.
    cmd=(npx wrangler deploy --env "$env_name")
    if [[ $dry_run -eq 1 ]]; then
      # [[REQ-646]] — a rehearsal builds no container image.
      cmd+=(--dry-run --containers-rollout=none)
    fi
    (cd "$app_dir" && "${cmd[@]}")
  fi
  deployed+=("$name")
done

  private async step(request: StepRequest, started: number): Promise<ContainerStepResult> {
    const compute = (reason: ExitReason, server: ServerCompute | null = null): StepCompute => ({
      peakMemoryMb: server?.peakMemoryMb ?? null,
      cpuS: server?.cpuS ?? null,
      wallS: (this.now() - started) / 1000,
      exitReason: reason,
      peakMemorySource: server?.peakMemorySource ?? null,
      memoryLimitMb: server?.memoryLimitMb ?? null,
    })
    let response: Response
    try {
      await this.ready(request.job.id)
      // RACED AGAINST THE CONTAINER'S OWN END. A request to a container that was
      // killed is not guaranteed to fail on its own; the container's exit, as the
      // platform reports it or as `running` turns false, is what ends the step.
      const stopped = this.stopped()
      try {
        response = await Promise.race([
          this.container.getTcpPort(CONTAINER_PORT).fetch('http://container/step', {
            method: 'POST',
            headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json' },
            body: JSON.stringify(request),
          }),
          stopped.promise,
        ])
      } finally {
        stopped.cancel()
      }
    } catch (err) {
      // THE CONTAINER STOPPED ANSWERING: ask the platform how it ended.
      const exit = await Promise.race([
        this.exited ?? Promise.resolve<ContainerExit>({}),
        new Promise<ContainerExit>((resolve) => setTimeout(() => resolve({}), EXIT_WITHIN_MS)),
      ])
      this.exited = null
      const why = err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)
      return { ok: false, message: `the job's container stopped: ${why}`, compute: compute(exitReason(exit)) }
    }

/** What a container is started with. */
export function containerEnv(env: Record<string, unknown>, job: string, token: string): Record<string, string> {
  const out: Record<string, string> = {}
  for (const name of [...MODEL_KEYS, ...CONTAINER_VARS]) {
    const value = env[name]
    if (typeof value === 'string' && value !== '') out[name] = value
  }
  // Present, so the host composes the capability, and worthless: the platform
  // attaches the real credential to the request on its way out.
  for (const name of EGRESS_SECRETS) {
    const value = env[name]
    if (typeof value === 'string' && value.trim() !== '') out[name] = HELD_SECRET
  }
  out.JOB_ID = job
  out.JOB_TOKEN = token
  out.PLATFORM_URL = `http://${PLATFORM_HOST}`
  out.PLATFORM_BOUND = boundNames(env).join(',')
  out.PORT = String(CONTAINER_PORT)
  const limit = memoryLimitMb(env)
```

{"content":"/**\n * [[BUG-359]] fix 1 — A JOB WHOSE WORKFLOW WENT QUIET LOSES ITS CONTAINER AND TOKEN.\n *\n * Only a successful `end` step used to stop a job's container. A Workflow killed\n * from outside, cancelled, or errored past its retries before `end` left the\n * container billing and its token able to call the platform as that job.\n *\n * The backstop is an alarm on the container's Durable Object, timed from\n * idleness: once a step has answered and no next one has started within\n * `IDLE_STOP` (five minutes; the Workflow calls again within a second), the\n * container is stopped and the token deleted. A step still in flight is left\n * alone until its own timeout.\n *\n * Driven through the real `JobContainer`, which the Durable Object hands its\n * container and storage; the platform's container and alarm are the doubles\n * (`fake-job-container.ts`), and the clock is the test's, so five minutes of\n * silence costs nothing to wait for.\n */\nimport { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'\nimport { IDLE_STOP_MS, INFLIGHT_STOP_MS } from '../apps/control-app/src/agent-container'\nimport { STEP_TIMEOUT_MS } from '../apps/control-app/src/job-step'\nimport { ROUND_STEP } from '../apps/control-app/src/turn-job'\nimport { jobUnderTest, roundRequest } from './support/fake-job-container'\n\ndescribe('BUG-359 — the stop backstop', () => {\n  it('test_UAT_FC_BUG-359_backstop_stops_abandoned_job_container', async () => {\n    const t = jobUnderTest()\n\n    // ONE STEP RUNS, AND THEN NOTHING: no next step, no `end` — a Workflow killed\n    // from outside.\n    const first = await t.job.runStep(roundRequest(0))\n    expect(first.ok).toBe(true)\n    const token = t.token()\n    expect(t.container.running).toBe(true)\n    expect(await t.job.verify(token)).toBe(true)\n\n    // THE BACKSTOP IS ARMED FOR IDLE_STOP after the step answered.\n    expect(IDLE_STOP_MS).toBe(5 * 60 * 1000)\n    expect(t.storage.alarmAt).toBe(t.now + IDLE_STOP_MS)\n\n    // A NEXT STEP WITHIN IDLE_STOP RE-ARMS IT; the container is the same one.\n    t.now += 1000\n    await t.job.runStep(roundRequest(1))\n    expect(t.container.starts).toBe(1)\n    expect(t.storage.alarmAt).toBe(t.now + IDLE_STOP_MS)\n\n    // AFTER IDLE_STOP OF SILENCE: the container is not running, and the job's\n    // token no longer authenticates.\n    await t.fireAlarm()\n    expect(t.container.running).toBe(false)\n    expect(await t.job.verify(token)).toBe(false)\n    expect(t.storage.alarmAt).toBeNull()\n\n    // A STEP ARRIVING AFTER THE BACKSTOP FIRED STARTS A FRESH CONTAINER, as a retry\n    // does: containers hold no state ([[DOC-1]] item 32). It is given a new token.\n    const late = await t.job.runStep(roundRequest(2))\n    expect(late.ok).toBe(true)\n    expect(t.container.starts).toBe(2)\n    expect(t.token()).not.toBe(token)\n    expect(await t.job.verify(t.token())).toBe(true)\n  })\n\n  it('test_UAT_FC_BUG-359_backstop_leaves_a_long_running_step_alone', async () => {\n    const t = jobUnderTest()\n    t.container.replies = [{ kind: 'hang' }]\n\n    // A ROUND STEP THAT RUNS FOR HOURS, as one may (its timeout is 2 hours).\n    const step = t.job.runStep(roundRequest(0))\n    await until(() => t.container.starts === 1 && t.storage.alarmAt !== null)\n    const startedAt = t.now\n    const token = t.token()\n    expect(t.storage.alarmAt).toBe(startedAt + INFLIGHT_STOP_MS)\n    expect(INFLIGHT_STOP_MS).toBeGreaterThan(STEP_TIMEOUT_MS)\n    expect(ROUND_STEP.timeout).toBe(STEP_TIMEOUT_MS)\n\n    // FAR PAST IDLE_STOP, STILL INSIDE THE STEP: an alarm firing now (say one set\n    // by an earlier step) leaves the container and its token alone, and is set\n    // again for when the step would be past its timeout.\n    t.now = startedAt + IDLE_STOP_MS * 6\n    await t.job.alarm()\n    expect(t.container.running).toBe(true)\n    expect(await t.job.verify(token)).toBe(true)\n    expect(t.storage.alarmAt).toBe(startedAt + INFLIGHT_STOP_MS)\n\n    // PAST THE STEP'S TIMEOUT the Workflow has given up on it: the container goes.\n    await t.fireAlarm()\n    expect(t.container.running).toBe(false)\n    expect(await t.job.verify(token)).toBe(false)\n\n    t.container.release()\n    await step\n  })\n})\n\nasync function until(ok: () => boolean, ms = 5000): Promise<void> {\n  const end = Date.now() + ms\n  while (!ok()) {\n    if (Date.now() > end) throw new Error('timed out waiting')\n    await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 10))\n  }\n}\n","file_path":"/Users/martin/.xgd/worktrees/git_github.com_lagrangefoundry_1stcontact.git/free-BUG-359/tests/test_UAT_FC_BUG-359_backstop_stops_abandoned_job_container.test.ts"}

Now every `end` attempt stops\n * both; a retry of `end` starts a fresh container to finish the job's\n * bookkeeping, which is correct because containers hold no state ([[DOC-1]]\n * item 32).\n *\n   const t = jobUnderTest()\n    await t.job.runStep(roundRequest(0))\n    const token = t.token()\n\n    // AN `end` WHOSE CONTAINER GOES MID-REQUEST (killed, or evicted).\n    t.container.replies = {"content":"/**\n * [[BUG-359]] fix 3 — A PRODUCTION DEPLOY PRUNES THE APP'S OLDER JOB IMAGES.\n *\n * Every production deploy pushes a ~1.5 GB job image to the account's Cloudflare\n * registry and nothing deleted the old ones. After a successful cloud deploy,\n * `bin/deploy` runs `1c images prune`, which keeps the image the app's container\n * application now runs plus the one before it (a one-version rollback) and\n * deletes the rest. A delete that fails warns; the deploy has already shipped.\n *\n * The registry and the Cloudflare API are stubbed at the HTTP boundary\n * (`fetch`): the account's container application, the Worker's versions, the\n * registry's credentials, its catalog, and its manifests. The app, its Worker\n * name and its container class are read from the real `wrangler.toml`.\n */\nimport { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process'\nimport { readFileSync } from 'node:fs'\nimport path from 'node:path'\nimport { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'\nimport { containerClasses, pruneRegistryImages } from '../tools/generate/src/cli/container-images'\n\nconst REPO = path.resolve(import.meta.dirname, '..')\nconst CONTROL_APP = path.join(REPO, 'apps', 'control-app')\nconst ACCOUNT = 'acct-bug359'\nconst REGISTRY = 'registry.cloudflare.com'\n\n/** The production Worker's name, as `wrangler.toml` gives it. */\nfunction productionWorker(): string {\n  const toml = readFileSync(path.join(CONTROL_APP, 'wrangler.toml'), 'utf8')\n  const block = toml.slice(toml.indexOf('[env.production]'))\n  return /^name\\s*=\\s*\"([^\"]+)\"/m.exec(block)![1]!\n}\n\n/** A Cloudflare account and registry holding `tags` for one container application. */\nfunction cloudflare(opts: { application: string; repository: string; deployed: string; tags: string[]; refuse?: string[] }) {\n  const tags = [...opts.tags]\n  const deletes: string[] = []\n  const uploaded = (tag: string) => `2026-11-0${opts.tags.indexOf(tag) + 1}T12:00:00Z`\n  const fetch = (async (input: string | URL, init?: RequestInit) => {\n    const url = String(input)\n    const method = init?.method ?? 'GET'\n    const ok = (result: unknown) => Response.json({ success: true, result })\n    if (url.endsWith(`/accounts/${ACCOUNT}/containers/applications`)) {\n      return ok([{ name: opts.application, configuration: { image: `${REGISTRY}/${ACCOUNT}/${opts.repository}:${opts.deployed}` } }])\n    }\n    if (url.includes(`/accounts/${ACCOUNT}/workers/scripts/`) && url.includes('/versions')) {\n      return ok({ items: opts.tags.map((t) => ({ id: `${t}-0000-0000-0000-000000000000`, metadata: { created_on: uploaded(t) } })) })\n    }\n    if (url.endsWith(`/containers/registries/${REGISTRY}/credentials`) && method === 'POST') return ok({ password: 'registry-password' })\n    if (url === `https://${REGISTRY}/v2/_catalog?tags=true`) return Response.json({ repositories: { [`${ACCOUNT}/${opts.repository}`]: [...tags] } })\n    const manifest = new RegExp(`^https://${REGISTRY}/v2/${ACCOUNT}/${opts.repository}/manifests/(.+)$`).exec(url)\n    if (manifest) {\n      const tag = manifest[1]!\n      if (!tags.includes(tag)) return new Response(null, { status: 404 })\n      if (method === 'DELETE') {\n        if (opts.refuse?.includes(tag)) return new Response(null, { status: 500 })\n        deletes.push(tag)\n        tags.splice(tags.indexOf(tag), 1)\n      }\n      return new Response(null, { status: 200 })\n    }\n    if (url === `https://${REGISTRY}/v2/gc/layers` && method === 'PUT') return new Response(null, { status: 202 })\n    return new Response(JSON.stringify({ success: false, errors: [{ message: `unexpected ${method} ${url}` }] }), { status: 404 })\n  }) as typeof globalThis.fetch\n  return { fetch, tags, deletes }\n}\n\ndescribe('BUG-359 — a production deploy prunes old registry images', () => {\n  const worker = productionWorker()\n  const classes = containerClasses(CONTROL_APP)\n  const application = `${worker}-${classes[0]!.toLowerCase()}`\n\n  it('test_UAT_FC_BUG-359_deploy_prunes_old_registry_images', async () => {\n    expect(classes).toEqual(['AgentContainer'])\n    // THREE OLDER IMAGES, the one before the deployed one, and the deployed one.\n    const cf = cloudflare({ application, repository: application, deployed: 'v5', tags: ['v1', 'v2', 'v3', 'v4', 'v5'] })\n\n    const [result] = await pruneRegistryImages({ token: 'token', worker, classes, env: { CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID: ACCOUNT }, fetch: cf.fetch })\n\n    // ONLY THE CURRENT IMAGE AND THE ONE BEFORE IT REMAIN.\n    expect(result!.skipped).toBeNull()\n    expect(result!.kept).toEqual(['v5', 'v4'])\n    expect(cf.tags.sort()).toEqual(['v4', 'v5'])\n    expect(cf.deletes.sort()).toEqual(['v1', 'v2', 'v3'])\n    expect(result!.failed).toEqual([])\n  })\n\n  it('test_UAT_FC_BUG-359_registry_prune_keeps_the_deployed_image_even_when_it_is_not_the_newest', async () => {\n    // A ROLLBACK: the deployed image is the previous one, a newer one exists.\n    const cf = cloudflare({ application, repository: application, deployed: 'v3', tags: ['v1', 'v2', 'v3', 'v4'] })\n    const [result] = await pruneRegistryImages({ token: 'token', worker, classes, env: { CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID: ACCOUNT }, fetch: cf.fetch })\n    expect(result!.kept[0]).toBe('v3')\n    expect(cf.tags).toContain('v3')\n    expect(cf.tags.length).toBe(2)\n  })\n\n  it('test_UAT_FC_BUG-359_a_failed_registry_delete_warns_and_never_throws', async () => {\n    const cf = cloudflare({ application, repository: application, deployed: 'v4', tags: ['v1', 'v2', 'v3', 'v4'], refuse: ['v1'] })\n    const [result] = await pruneRegistryImages({ token: 'token', worker, classes, env: { CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID: ACCOUNT }, fetch: cf.fetch })\n **Result**

```
function send(res: http.ServerResponse, status: number, body: unknown): Promise<void> {
  return new Promise((resolve) => {
    if (res.writableEnded) return resolve()
    res.writeHead(status, { 'content-type': 'application/json' })
    res.end(JSON.stringify(body), () => resolve())
  })
}

function readBody(req: http.IncomingMessage): Promise<string> {
  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    const chunks: Buffer[] = []
    req.on('data', (c: Buffer) => chunks.push(c))
    req.on('end', () => resolve(Buffer.concat(chunks).toString('utf8')))
    req.on('error', reject)
  })
}

/** Start the job's server. Exported for the suites, which run it in-process. */
export function startJobServer(opts: {
  vars: Record<string, string | undefined>
  /** Exit the process: `process.exit` in the container. */
  exit: (code: number) => void
  port?: number
  /** How a step is run: `executeStep` over the platform. The suites replace it to measure memory alone. */
  execute?: (request: StepRequest) => Promise<StepAnswer>
  /** How the container's memory is read ([[BUG-359]]); `containerMemoryProbe()` by default. */
  memory?: MemoryProbe
  /** How often a step's memory is sampled; about a second by default. */
  sampleEveryMs?: number
}): Promise<{ server: http.Server; port: number; stop(signal: string): Promise<void> }> {
  const vars = Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(opts.vars).filter((e): e is [string, string] => typeof e[1] === 'string'))
  const job = vars.JOB_ID ?? ''
  const token = vars.JOB_TOKEN ?? ''
  const base = vars.PLATFORM_URL ?? ''
  if (job === '' || token === '' || base === '') throw new Error('the job container was started without JOB_ID, JOB_TOKEN and PLATFORM_URL')
  const direct = globalThis.fetch.bind(globalThis)
  const transport = platformTransport(base, job, token, direct)
  // THE PROCESS'S `fetch`: an account or provider API goes through the platform,
  // which attaches the credential this process is never given.
  globalThis.fetch = platformFetch(transport, vars, direct)
  const bound = (vars.PLATFORM_BOUND ?? '').split(',').filter((name) => name !== '')
  const env = remoteEnv(transport, bound, vars) as never
  const launch = chromiumLauncher(vars.CHROMIUM_PATH)
  const limitMb = Number(vars.JOB_MEMORY_LIMIT_MB) > 0 ? Number(vars.JOB_MEMORY_LIMIT_MB) : null
  const enforce = vars.JOB_MEMORY_ENFORCE === '1' && limitMb !== null
  const probe = opts.memory ?? containerMemoryProbe()

  return new Promise((resolve) => {
    server.listen(opts.port ?? Number(vars.PORT ?? 8080), '0.0.0.0', () => {
      const address = server.address()
      resolve({ server, port: typeof address === 'object' && address ? address.port : 0, stop })
    })
  })
}

/** The container's entry: run until SIGTERM. */
async function main(): Promise<void> {
  const { stop } = await startJobServer({ vars: process.env, exit: (code) => process.exit(code) })
  process.on('SIGTERM', () => void stop('SIGTERM'))
  process.on('SIGINT', () => void stop('SIGINT'))
}

if (process.env.JOB_CONTAINER_MAIN === '1') void main()
```

async function freeBandPort(from: number): Promise<number> {
  for (let port = from; port < 8900; port += 1) {
    const free = await new Promise<boolean>((resolve) => {
      const probe = net.createServer()
      probe.once('error', () => resolve(false))
      probe.once('listening', () => probe.close(() => resolve(true)))
      probe.listen(port, '127.0.0.1')
    })
    if (free) {
      expect(inDevPortBand(port)).toBe(true)
      return port
    }
  }
  throw new Error('no free port in the dev band')
}

/** A stand-in service: the real NAME, a free port, and a process that binds it. */
function standIn(service: DevService, port: number): DevService {
  return {
    ...service,
    port,
    argv: [process.execPath, '-e', `require('node:net').createServer().listen(${port}, '127.0.0.1')`],
  }
}

/** A snapshot manifest on disk, so `up` can report what is being served. */
function writeSnapshot(root: string, app: string, over: Partial<DevSnapshot> = {}): DevSnapshot {
  const snapshot: DevSnapshot = {
    app,
    env: 'dev',
    worker: `1stcontact-${app}-dev`,
    entry: 'worker/worker.js',
    config: 'wrangler.toml',
    assets: null,
    deployedAt: '2026-09-26T09:15:00Z',
    commit: 'cafe123',
    ...over,
  }
  const dir = snapshotDir(root, app)
  fs.mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true })
  fs.writeFileSync(path.join(dir, SNAPSHOT_MANIFEST), `${JSON.stringify(snapshot, null, 2)}\n`)
  return snapshot
}

/** The package manifests the ticket names, parsed. */
function manifest(rel: string): { scripts?: Record<string, string> } {
  return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(REPO, rel), 'utf8')) as { scripts?: Record<string, string> }
}

{"content":"/**\n * [[BUG-359]] fix 7 — A DEV START REMOVES THE APP'S OLDER LOCAL JOB IMAGES.\n *\n * Every dev-worker start on a new snapshot has `wrangler dev` build a new\n * `cloudflare-dev/<class>:<tag>` image (~1.5 GB) and nothing removed the old ones:\n * six built up in a day. Now, after a start succeeds, `devUp` keeps the tags that\n * appeared during the start (what is being served) and `docker rmi`s the older\n * ones, never touching an image a running container uses.\n *\n * REAL DOCKER, SCRATCH IMAGES. The repo is a temporary one whose control app's\n * `wrangler.toml` runs a container class of its own (`Bug359Probe`), so the prune\n * only ever sees `cloudflare-dev/bug359probe:*` — never the operator's real job\n * images. The images are tags of an image already present (no pull, no disk),\n * and the stand-in dev worker tags the new one as it starts, as wrangler builds\n * one before it is ready.\n */\nimport { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process'\nimport fs from 'node:fs'\nimport net from 'node:net'\nimport os from 'node:os'\nimport path from 'node:path'\nimport { afterAll, afterEach, beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest'\nimport { DEV_SERVICES, devUp, formatUp, inDevPortBand, type DevService } from '../tools/generate/src/cli/dev'\nimport { realDocker } from '../tools/generate/src/cli/container-images'\n\nconst REPO_PROBE = 'cloudflare-dev/bug359probe'\n\nfunction docker(args: string[], allowFail = false): string {\n  try {\n    return execFileSync('docker', args, { encoding: 'utf8', stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'] }).trim()\n  } catch (err) {\n    if (allowFail) return ''\n    throw err\n  }\n}\n\nconst DOCKER = docker(['info', '--format', '{{.ServerVersion}}'], true) !== ''\n\n/** An image already present to tag from: no pull, no new layers. */\nfunction baseImage(): string | null {\n  const present = docker(['images', '--format', '{{.Repository}}:{{.Tag}}'], true).split('\\n')\n  return present.find((ref) => ref === 'node:22-slim') ?? present.find((ref) => ref.startsWith('node:') && !ref.endsWith('<none>')) ?? null\n}\n\nfunction probeTags(): string[] {\n  return docker(['images', REPO_PROBE, '--format', '{{.Repository}}:{{.Tag}}'], true)\n    .split('\\n')\n    .filter((l) => l !== '')\n    .sort()\n}\n\nfunction cleanUp(): void {\n  const containers = docker(['ps', '-aq', '--filter', 'label=bug359probe=1'], true).split('\\n').filter(Boolean)\n  if (containers.length > 0) docker(['rm', '-f', ...containers], true)\n  for (const ref of probeTags()) docker(['rmi', ref], true)\n}\n\n/** A temporary repo whose control app runs one container class: `Bug359Probe`. */\nfunction tempRepo(): string {\n  const root = fs.mkdtempSync(path.join(os.tmpdir(), 'bug359-dev-'))\n  const app = path.join(root, 'apps', 'control-app')\n  fs.mkdirSync(app, { recursive: true })\n  fs.writeFileSync(\n    path.join(app, 'wrangler.toml'),\n    ['name = \"bug359-probe\"', '', '[[containers]]', 'class_name = \"Bug359Probe\"', 'image = \"./Dockerfile\"', 'instance_type = \"lite\"', ''].join('\\n'),\n  )\n  return root\n}\n\nasync function freeBandPort(from: number): Promise<number> {\n  for (let port = from; port < 8900; port += 1) {\n    const free = await new Promise<boolean>((resolve) => {\n      const probe = net.createServer()\n      probe.once('error', () => resolve(false))\n      probe.once('listening', () => probe.close(() => resolve(true)))\n      probe.listen(port, '127.0.0.1')\n    })\n    if (free && inDevPortBand(port)) return port\n  }\n  throw new Error('no free port in the dev band')\n}\n\nconst children: number[] = []\n\n/** The dev worker, standing in: builds its image (tags `newTag`), then listens. */\nasync function devWorker(base: string, newTag: string): Promise<DevService> {\n  const real = DEV_SERVICES.find((s) => s.name === 'dev-worker')!\n  const port = await freeBandPort(8860)\n  const script = [\n    \"const { execFileSync } = require('node:child_process')\",\n    `execFileSync('docker', ['tag', ${JSON.stringify(base)}, ${JSON.stringify(`${REPO_PROBE}:${newTag}`)}])`,\n    `require('node:net').createServer().listen(${port}, '127.0.0.1')`,\n  ].join('\\n')\n  return { ...real, port, argv: [process.execPath, '-e', script] }\n}\n\nlet base: string | null = null\n\nbeforeAll(() => {\n  if (!DOCKER) return\n  base = baseImage()\n  cleanUp()\n})\n\nafterEach(() => {\n  for (const pid of children.splice(0)) {\n    try {\n      process.kill(pid, 'SIGKILL')\n    } catch {\n      // Already gone.\n    }\n  }\n})\n\nafterAll(() => {\n  if (DOCKER) cleanUp()\n})\n\ndescribe('BUG-359 — a dev start prunes the older local job images', () => {\n  it.runIf(DOCKER)('test_UAT_FC_BUG-359_dev_start_prunes_old_job_images', async () => {\n    expect(base, 'no local node image to tag the scratch images from').not.toBeNull()\n    // TWO OLDER TAGGED IMAGES, left by earlier starts.\n    docker(['tag', base!, `${REPO_PROBE}:old-1`])\n    docker(['tag', base!, `${REPO_PROBE}:old-2`])\n    expect(probeTags()).toEqual([`${REPO_PROBE}:old-1`, `${REPO_PROBE}:old-2`])\n\n    const root = tempRepo()\n    const outcome = await devUp({ repoRoot: root, services: [await devWorker(base!, 'new-1')], timeoutMs: 30_000, docker: realDocker })\n    for (const s of outcome.started) children.push(s.pid)\n    expect(outcome.ok, formatUp(outcome)).toBe(true)\n\n    // ONLY THE ONE NOW BEING SERVED REMAINS.\n    expect(probeTags()).toEqual([`${REPO_PROBE}:new-1`])\n    const pruned = outcome.images?.find((i) => i.app === 'control-app')?.result\n    expect(pruned?.kept).toEqual([`${REPO_PROBE}:new-1`])\n    expect(pruned?.removed.sort()).toEqual([`${REPO_PROBE}:old-1`, `${REPO_PROBE}:old-2`])\n    // And the start says so.\n    expect(formatUp(outcome)).toContain(`removed ${REPO_PROBE}:old-`)\n    fs.r

/**
 * [[BUG-359]] — WHAT THE WHOLE JOB CONTAINER USES, AND WHAT IT IS ALLOWED.
 *
 * The step's peak memory used to be the Node server's own `maxRSS`, which leaves
 * out Chromium: Chromium runs as child processes and counts against the instance's
 * limit all the same (the live container used 1.7 GB while its row said 854 MB).
 * So the peak is now the container's:
 *
 *  - `cgroup` — the container's own memory cgroup, where there is one: its usage
 *    less the inactive file cache the kernel reclaims before it kills anything
 *    (what `docker stats` reports). A root cgroup has no `memory.current`, so a
 *    cgroup that would cover the whole machine (a VM's) is never read as this.
 *  - `process_tree` — otherwise, the summed resident set of the server's whole
 *    process tree, from `/proc` (or `ps` where there is no `/proc`).
 *
 * NEVER THE NODE PROCESS'S OWN `maxRSS`, which is the bug. The figure says which
 * of the two produced it.
 *
 * Sampled about once a second while a step runs, because neither source keeps a
 * per-step peak: `memory.peak` is the cgroup's lifetime high-water mark, and a
 * process tree has none.
 */
import { execFile } from 'node:child_process'
import { existsSync } from 'node:fs'
import { readFile, readdir } from 'node:fs/promises'
import path from 'node:path'

export type PeakMemorySource = 'cgroup' | 'process_tree'

/** One way of reading the container's memory now, MB. */
export interface MemoryProbe {
  readonly source: PeakMemorySource
  /** The memory in use now, MB; `null` when it could not be read this time. */
  read(): Promise<number | null>
}

const MB = 1024 * 1024

/** The usage, and the stat whose `inactive_file` is reclaimable, of a cgroup v2 or v1 hierarchy. */
function cgroupFiles(root: string): { usage: string; stat: string; inactive: string } | null {
  const v2 = path.join(root, 'memory.current')
  if (existsSync(v2)) return { usage: v2, stat: path.join(root, 'memory.stat'), inactive: 'inactive_file' }
  const v1 = path.join(root, 'memory', 'memory.usage_in_bytes')
  if (existsSync(v1)) return { usage: v1, stat: path.join(root, 'memory', 'memory.stat'), inactive: 'total_inactive_file' }
  return null
}

/** The container's cgroup, where one covers just the container; else `null`. */
export function cgroupProbe(root = '/sys/fs/cgroup'): MemoryProbe | null {
  const files = cgroupFiles(root)
  if (!files) return null
  return {
    source: 'cgroup',
    read: async () => {
      try {
        const usage = Number((await readFile(files.usage, 'utf8')).trim())
        if (!Number.isFinite(usage)) return null
        const stat = await readFile(files.stat, 'utf8').catch(() => '')
        const line = stat.split('\n').find((l) => l.startsWith(`${files.inactive} `))
        const inactive = line ? Number(line.split(' ')[1]) : 0
        return Math.max(0, usage - (Number.isFinite(inactive) ? inactive : 0)) / MB
      } catch {
        return null
      }
    },
  }
}

/** pid → [parent pid, resident KiB], for every process this one can see. */
type ProcessTable = Map<number, [number, number]>

async function procTable(): Promise<ProcessTable> {
  const table: ProcessTable = new Map()
  for (const entry of await readdir('/proc')) {
    if (!/^\d+$/.test(entry)) continue
    try {
      const status = await readFile(`/proc/${entry}/status`, 'utf8')
      const ppid = /^PPid:\s+(\d+)/m.exec(status)
      const rss = /^VmRSS:\s+(\d+)\s*kB/m.exec(status)
      table.set(Number(entry), [ppid ? Number(ppid[1]) : 0, rss ? Number(rss[1]) : 0])
    } catch {
      // A process that ended between the listing and the read.
    }
  }
  return table
}

function psTable(): Promise<ProcessTable> {
  return new Promise((resolve) => {
    execFile('ps', ['-A', '-o', 'pid=,ppid=,rss='], { maxBuffer: 16 * MB }, (err, stdout) => {
      const table: ProcessTable = new Map()
      if (!err) {
        for (const line of stdout.split('\n')) {
          const [pid, ppid, rss] = line.trim().split(/\s+/).map(Number)
          if (Number.isFinite(pid) && Number.isFinite(ppid) && Number.isFinite(rss)) table.set(pid!, [ppid!, rss!])
        }
      }
      resolve(table)
    })
  })
}

/** The summed resident set of `root` and every process descended from it, MB. */
export function treeRssMb(table: ProcessTable, root: number): number | null {
  if (!table.has(root)) return null
  const children = new Map<number, number[]>()
  for (const [pid, [ppid]] of table) {
    if (pid === root) continue
    const list = children.get(ppid) ?? []
    list.push(pid)
    children.set(ppid, list)
  }
  let kib = 0
  const queue = [root]
  const seen = new Set<number>()
  while (queue.length > 0) {
    const pid = queue.pop()!
    if (seen.has(pid)) continue
    seen.add(pid)
    kib += table.get(pid)?.[1] ?? 0
    queue.push(...(children.get(pid) ?? []))
  }
  return kib / 1024
}

/** The summed resident set of the process tree rooted at `root` (this process by default). */
export function processTreeProbe(root = process.pid): MemoryProbe {
  const proc = existsSync('/proc/self/status')
  return {
    source: 'process_tree',
    read: async () => treeRssMb(await (proc ? procTable() : psTable()), root),
  }
}

/** The container's cgroup where one covers just it, else its process tree. */
export function containerMemoryProbe(): MemoryProbe {
  return cgroupProbe() ?? processTreeProbe()
}

/** One step's peak, and how it was measured. */
export interface StepMemory {
  peakMb: number | null
  source: PeakMemorySource
}

/**
 * Sample `probe` from now until `stop()`, about every `everyMs`, keeping the peak.
 * `over` is called once, with the sample, the first time one goes past `limitMb`.
 */
export function sampleStep(
  probe: MemoryProbe,
  opts: { everyMs?: number; limitMb?: number
```

import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process'
import { mkdtempSync, readdirSync, rmSync, statSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs'
import { DatabaseSync } from 'node:sqlite'
import os from 'node:os'
import path from 'node:path'
import { getPlatformProxy, unstable_dev, type Unstable_DevWorker } from 'wrangler'
import { sitePlan } from '../../apps/control-app/src/plan'
import { ticketStoreFor } from '../../apps/control-app/src/tickets'

import { nextSlug, siteSeed } from './site-seed'

/**
 * [[REQ-646]] — THE CONTROL APP UNDER `wrangler dev`, WITH ITS JOB CONTAINERS.
 *
 * The real Worker (`worker.ts`, its own `wrangler.toml`), the real
 * `ConsultantTurn` Workflow, the real `AgentContainer` Durable Object, and the
 * job's real container — the image `container/Dockerfile` builds, run by Docker
 * the way `wrangler dev` runs it for local development ([[DOC-86]] §6). Nothing
 * inside the platform is replaced. The one double is the model, served over HTTP
 * (`model-server.ts`), because the container builds its own client.
 *
 * SEEDED THROUGH THE STORES, NOT THROUGH ROUTES. There is no route that creates a
 * site, so the tenant, its site, its Library and its products are written with
 * the store code itself, through `getPlatformProxy`, into the same local
 * persistence the dev server then serves.
 *
 * DOCKER IS REQUIRED, as it is for `bin/dev` and `bin/deploy`.
 */

type Untyped = any // eslint-disable-line @typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any

export const REPO = path.resolve(import.meta.dirname, '..', '..')
const APP = path.join(REPO, 'apps', 'control-app')
const WRANGLER = path.join(REPO, 'node_modules', '.bin', 'wrangler')

/** The image `wrangler dev` builds for the `AgentContainer` class. */
export const JOB_IMAGE = 'cloudflare-dev/agentcontainer'

export function docker(args: string[], opts: { allowFail?: boolean } = {}): string {
  try {
    return execFileSync('docker', args, { encoding: 'utf8', stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'] }).trim()
  } catch (err) {
    if (opts.allowFail) return ''
    throw err
  }
}

/** Whether a Docker engine answers. */
export function dockerRunning(): boolean {
  return docker(['info', '--format', '{{.ServerVersion}}'], { allowFail: true }) !== ''
}

 * [[BUG-359]] — the name every container workerd starts for the rig's Worker

import { JobContainer, type ContainerLike, type TokenStorage } from '../../apps/control-app/src/agent-container'
import type { StepRequest } from '../../apps/control-app/src/job-step'

/**
 * [[BUG-359]] — `JobContainer` AS ITS DURABLE OBJECT DRIVES IT, with the platform's
 * two halves replaced: the container (`ctx.container`) and the object's storage
 * with its alarm (`ctx.storage`). Everything between them — the step clock, the
 * backstop's alarm, `end`, the token — is the real code.
 *
 * The clock is the test's, so "five minutes of silence" is a number, not a wait.
 */

/** How the fake container answers a `POST /step`. */
export type StepReply =
  | { kind: 'answer'; body: unknown }
  | { kind: 'error'; status: number; body: unknown }
  /** Never answers until `release()` is called: a step still running. */
  | { kind: 'hang' }

export interface FakeContainer extends ContainerLike {
  /** How many times the container was started. */
  starts: number
  /** The env each start was given, in order. */
  envs: Record<string, string>[]
  /** The next replies to `/step`, consumed in order; the last repeats. */
  replies: StepReply[]
  /** Answer the step that is hanging. */
  release(body?: unknown): void
}

export function fakeContainer(): FakeContainer {
  let running = false
  let exit: ((value?: unknown) => void) | null = null
  let hung: ((r: Response) => void) | null = null
  const c: FakeContainer = {
    starts: 0,
    envs: [],
    replies: [{ kind: 'answer', body: { answer: { ended: true }, compute: { peakMemoryMb: 100, cpuS: 0.1 } } }],
    get running() {
      return running
    },
    start(options) {
      running = true
      c.starts++
      c.envs.push(options.env)
    },
    monitor() {
      return new Promise((resolve) => {
        exit = resolve
      })
    },
    async destroy() {
      running = false
      exit?.()
      exit = null
    },
    getTcpPort() {
      return {
        fetch: async (input: string) => {
          if (input.endsWith('/health')) return new Response('ok')
          const reply = c.replies.length > 1 ? c.replies.shift()! : c.replies[0]!
          if (reply.kind === 'answer') return Response.json(reply.body)
          if (reply.kind === 'error') return Response.json(reply.body, { status: reply.status })
          return new Promise<Response>((resolve) => {
            hung = resolve
          })
        },
      }
    },
    release(body = { answer: { outcome: { kind: 'done' } }, compute: { peakMemoryMb: 100, cpuS: 0.1 } }) {
      hung?.(Response.json(body))
      hung = null
    },
  }
  return c
}

/** Durable Object storage, with the one alarm an object has. */
export interface FakeStorage extends TokenStorage {
  data: Map<string, unknown>
  /** When the alarm is set for, or `null` when none is. */
  alarmAt: number | null
}

export function fakeStorage(): FakeStorage {
  const s: FakeStorage = {
    data: new Map(),
    alarmAt: null,
    get: async <T>(key: string) => s.data.get(key) as T | undefined,
    put: async (key, value) => {
      s.data.set(key, structuredClone(value))
    },
    delete: async (key) => s.data.delete(key),
    setAlarm: async (at) => {
      s.alarmAt = at
    },
    deleteAlarm: async () => {
      s.alarmAt = null
    },
  }
  return s
}

/** A job's container, its storage and a clock the test moves. */
export interface JobUnderTest {
  job: JobContainer
  container: FakeContainer
  storage: FakeStorage
  /** The clock `JobContainer` reads, ms. */
  now: number
  /** Move the clock to the alarm and fire it, as the platform does. */
  fireAlarm(): Promise<void>
  /** The job's token as the container was started with it. */
  token(): string
}

export function jobUnderTest(env: Record<string, unknown> = {}): JobUnderTest {
  const container = fakeContainer()
  const storage = fakeStorage()
  const t: JobUnderTest = {
    container,
    storage,
    now: Date.parse('2026-11-05T12:00:00Z'),
    job: null as unknown as JobContainer,
    fireAlarm: async () => {
      if (storage.alarmAt === null) throw new Error('no alarm is set')
      t.now = Math.max(t.now, storage.alarmAt)
      storage.alarmAt = null
      await t.job.alarm()
    },
    token: () => container.envs.at(-1)?.JOB_TOKEN ?? '',
  }
  t.job = new JobContainer(container, storage, env, {}, () => t.now)
  return t
}

  scope: { tenantId: 'tenant-bug359', siteId: 'site-bug359' },
  origin: 'http://127.0.0.1',
} as unknown as Pick<StepRequest, 'job' | 'scope' | 'origin'>

export function roundRequest(step = 0): StepRequest {
  return { ...place, kind: 'round', step, state: {} as never, userId: null } as StepRequest
}

export function endRequest(): StepRequest {
  return { ...place, kind: 'end', end: { kind: 'done', content: 'Done.' } } as StepRequest
}

```
 * [[REQ-646]] — how one job step's container ended. `ok` the step answered;
 * `error` the step threw and the container lived; `sigterm` a deploy or host move
 * that the container met with a checkpoint; `oom` a memory kill (an OOM or
 * `exceededMemory`), told apart from a SIGTERM; `crash` any other death.
 */
export type ExitReason = 'ok' | 'error' | 'sigterm' | 'oom' | 'crash'

/** [[REQ-646]] — what one job step's container used, as it reported or was seen to. */
export interface StepCompute {
  /** Peak resident set, MB; `null` where the container died before it could say. */
  peakMemoryMb: number | null
  /** CPU seconds (user + system); `null` where unknown. */
  cpuS: number | null
  /** Wall seconds the step ran, as the runner timed it. */
  wallS: number
  exitReason: ExitReason
  /** [[BUG-359]] — how the peak was measured; `null` where it was not. */
  peakMemorySource?: PeakMemorySource | null
  /** [[BUG-359]] — the instance type's memory limit the peak was compared with, MB. */
  memoryLimitMb?: number | null
}

/**
 * [[BUG-359]] — how a step's peak memory was measured: the container's own cgroup,
 * or the summed resident set of the server's whole process tree, Chromium included.
 * Never the Node process's own `maxRSS`, which left Chromium out.
 */
export type PeakMemorySource = 'cgroup' | 'process_tree'

/** One row, as the operator surface reads it. */
export interface TurnLogRow {
  turn: string
  session: string
  startedAt: string
  endedAt: string | null
  outcome: TurnLogOutcome | null
  detail: string | null
  /** [[BUG-288]] — the turn's last sign of life; `null` on a row older than the stamp. */
  aliveAt: string | null
  /** [[BUG-288]] — when the Worker version that opened the turn was deployed, if known. */
  deployedAt: string | null
}

/**
 * Open a turn's row, and hand back the means to close it.
 *
 * THE CALLER AWAITS THIS BEFORE IT RETURNS A `Response`, and that ordering is
 * the entire guarantee. Awaited inside the stream it would be one more thing the
 * dying isolate failed to do.
 *
 * ONE STATEMENT, NO READ. The row is a fact about a moment that has already
 * happened, so there is nothing to fold and nothing to compare — which matters
 * because this runs on the critical path of a turn the customer is waiting for.
 *
 * A FAILED OPEN IS `null` AND NOT A THROW. See the header: the turn proceeds
 * unrecorded rather than not at all.
 */
export async function openTurn(
  env: TurnLogEnv | null,
  tenantId: string,
  sessionId: string,
  now: () => string = () => new Date().toISOString(),
  /** [[BUG-288]] — the running Worker version's deploy time, where the runtime says. */
  deployedAt: string | null = null,
): Promise<OpenTurn | null> {
  if (!env?.DB) return null
  const id = newId('turn')
  const started = now()
  try {
    await env.DB.prepare(
      'INSERT INTO turn_log (turn_id, tenant_id, session_id, started_at, alive_at, deployed_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)',
    )
      .bind(id, tenantId, sessionId, started, started, deployedAt)
      .run()
  } catch {
    // See the header: an operator loses a record, the customer keeps their turn.
    return null
  }
  return turnRow(env, id, now)
}

/**
 * [[REQ-624]] — an open row by its id: what a consultant job beats and closes from
 * wherever its steps run, the route that opened the row long gone.
 */
export function turnRow(env: TurnLogEnv, id: string, now: () => string = () => new Date().toISOString()): OpenTurn {
  return {
    id,
    alive: async () => {
      try {
        // ONLY WHILE OPEN: a stamp racing the close must not reopen nothing, and
        // a closed row's last sign of life is its `ended_at`.
        await env.DB.prepare('UPDATE turn_log SET alive_at = ? WHERE turn_id = ? AND ended_at IS NULL')
          .bind(now(), id)
          .run()
      } catch {
        // A missed stamp is one beat of five; the next one carries it.
      }
    },
    compute: async (step) => {
      try {
        await env.DB.prepare(
          // [[BUG-359]] — the peak's source is the source of the step that set the
          // peak: SET reads the row as it was, so both see the old peak.
          'UPDATE turn_log SET peak_memory_source = CASE WHEN ? IS NOT NULL AND ? IS NOT NULL AND ? >= COALESCE(peak_memory_mb, 0) THEN ? ELSE peak_memory_source END, memory_limit_mb = COALESCE(?, memory_limit_mb), peak_memory_mb = MAX(COALESCE(peak_memory_mb, 0), COALESCE(?, 0)), cpu_s = COALESCE(cpu_s, 0) + COALESCE(?, 0), wall_s = COALESCE(wall_s, 0) + ?, exit_reason = ?, memory_kills = memory_kills + ? WHERE turn_id = ?',
        )
          .bind(
            step.peakMemorySource ?? null,
            step.peakMemoryMb,
            step.peakMemoryMb,
            step.peakMemorySource ?? null,
            step.memoryLimitMb ?? null,
            step.peakMemoryMb,
            step.cpuS,
            step.wallS,
            step.exitReason,
            step.exitReason === 'oom' ? 1 : 0,
            id,
          )
          .run()
      } catch {
        // An unrecorded step costs the operator one measurement, never the turn.
      }
    },
    close: async (outcome, detail) => {
      try {
        await env.DB.prepare(
          'UPDATE turn_log SET ended_at = ?, outcome = ?, detail = ? WHERE turn_id = ?',
        )
          // NULL AND NOT AN EMPTY STRING for a turn with nothing to explain: an
          // empty string would claim it had said something.

-- [[BUG-359]] — HOW A TURN'S PEAK MEMORY WAS MEASURED, AND WHAT IT WAS HELD TO.
--
-- `peak_memory_mb` ([[REQ-646]], `0048`) was the Node server's own `maxRSS`,
-- which leaves out Chromium: the job container used 1.7 GB while the row said
-- 854 MB. The peak is now the whole container's — its cgroup where one covers
-- just the container, else the summed resident set of the server's process
-- tree — and `peak_memory_source` says which (`cgroup` or `process_tree`).
--
-- `memory_limit_mb` is the job's instance type's limit the peak was compared
-- with. In dev and the container suites a step over it fails as `oom`; in
-- production the platform enforces the limit, and this column is the record of
-- how near a turn came to it. NULL on rows written before this file.
--
-- `memory_limit_mb` LAST, because the test harness's `atHead` marker asks about
-- the last statement of the last file.
ALTER TABLE turn_log ADD COLUMN peak_memory_source TEXT;
ALTER TABLE turn_log ADD COLUMN memory_limit_mb REAL;
